       Local and wrapped geometry inside the
                               Hall algebra
Define the completed degree-zero and positive-degree pieces

                 H𝑋loc = c 𝑚 ( 𝛾 )=0 𝐾0𝐸 (St/M 𝛾 ) ,         H𝑋wr = c 𝑚 ( 𝛾 ) >0 𝐾0𝐸 (St/M 𝛾 ) .
                         M                                          M


A pure one-dimensional support of degree zero projects to a finite subset of 𝐸 . A support of positive
degree projects onto 𝐸 . The first sector is point-local on the elliptic curve; the second is wrapped.

Theorem 112.1 (Geometric local–wrapped decomposition). Hall multiplication respects elliptic degree:

                                            H𝑚1 ∗ H𝑚2 ⊆ H𝑚1 +𝑚2 .

Consequently H𝑋loc is a subalgebra, H𝑋wr is a closed two-sided ideal, and

                                            H𝑋𝐸,mot  H𝑋loc ⋉ H𝑋wr

as a complete vector space with multiplication determined by the Hall correspondences. The reduced inte-
gration morphism preserves this decomposition.
Proof. The cycle of the middle term of a short exact sequence is the sum of the cycles of its subobject and quotient.
Projection to 𝐸 therefore adds degrees. Degree zero is closed under addition, and positive degree absorbs addition
by a nonnegative degree. The same grading is present on the target group algebra of 𝐼𝑋𝐸 .

   This theorem supplies the geometric version of the local–wrapped splitting constructed from the
Borcherds root algebra in the companion volume. The two constructions have the same additive elliptic
degree and become comparable after a primitive root framing is chosen.

with its cohomological Hall product.

Theorem 113.1 (Strong-oriented cohomological lift). For every strong orientation enhancement on the
𝐸 -equivariant derived moduli problem, the attractor correspondence constructs an associative cohomological
Hall algebra H𝑋𝐸,coh . Elliptic degree again gives a subalgebra in degree zero and a closed ideal in positive
degree. Forgetting the cohomological coeﬃcient system recovers the corresponding motivic Hall correspon-
dences.
Proof. The parabolic-induction theorem of Kinjo–Park–Safronov gives the cohomological product from the
strong-oriented attractor Lagrangian correspondence [52]. Numerical charge and elliptic degree are constant on
connected components of every correspondence, so the proof of the preceding decomposition applies verbatim.

Theorem 113.2 (Exact geometric character map). The map 𝐼𝑋𝐸,Π is multiplicative. Its logarithm is primi-
tive for the completed Hall coproduct whenever that coproduct is deﬁned in the selected localization. In every
reduced Donaldson–Thomas sector covered by the equivariant integration theorem, its coeﬃcients are the
reduced invariants of 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 . The primitive reduced Gromov–Witten theory supplies the independent
automorphic character − 𝜒10−1 in classes primitive in the K3 factor.
Proof. Multiplicativity of 𝐼𝑋𝐸 is the reduced integration theorem. The cocycle identity for the polarization cross-
term makes the graph of Π multiplicative. The logarithm of a group-like series is primitive. The enumerative
identifications are the reduced Hall calculations of Oberdieck–Shen and the primitive Igusa theorem of Oberdieck–
Pixton [64, 65].

   The geometric Hall source and the universal Igusa avatar now occupy explicit objects. A primitive
root framing is a morphism from the simple BPS sheaves of H𝑋𝐸,coh to the simple-root spaces of 𝔤Δ5
that preserves the Euler pairing, parity, and extension brackets. The universal presentation theorem then
extends this framing through Hall, current, quantum-double, and determinant constructions.

                                                                       BPS primitives
A connected graded bialgebra has primitive Lie algebra

                                 Prim( 𝐻 ) = { 𝑥 : Δ𝑥 = 𝑥 ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ 𝑥 } .

In cohomological Donaldson–Thomas theory, the BPS sheaf isolates the primitive part of the Hall
algebra.

Theorem 114.1 (Two-Calabi–Yau BPS algebra). For the geometric two-Calabi–Yau categories covered by
the BPS-algebra theorem, the BPS algebra is the positive enveloping algebra of a generalized Kac–Moody
Lie algebra. This scope includes semistable sheaves on K3 and other symplectic surfaces under the stated
good-moduli hypotheses.
Proof. The theorem is the Davison–Hennecart–Schlegel Mejia construction [23]. The simple BPS sheaves give
the simple-root spaces, extension correspondences give the bracket, and the integrality/PBW theorem identifies
the Hall algebra with the positive enveloping algebra.

   This result supplies a global K3 generalized Kac–Moody algebra at the two-Calabi–Yau Hall level.
Yangian structures arise from additional equivariant correspondences and coproducts in the surface and
quiver models described below.

         The integral Hall–Borcherds amalgam
Let 𝔭+𝑋 be a complete charge-graded BPS Lie superalgebra supplied by a geometric Hall source, and
let 𝔫Δ5 ,+ be the positive Igusa Borcherds Lie superalgebra. The polarized charge extension places both
gradings over one abelian Cartan algebra 𝔥Π . Form the two complete Borels

                                  𝔟 𝑋 = 𝔥Π ⋉ 𝔭+𝑋 ,          𝔟Δ = 𝔥Π ⋉ 𝔫Δ5 ,+ .

The Cartan acts through the physical charge on the first Borel and through Gram degree on the second.

Definition 115.1 (Integral protected Lie algebra). The integral Hall–Borcherds algebra is the completed
amalgamated coproduct
                                                        ⨿𝔥Π 𝔟Δ
                                           𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝔟 𝑋 b
in complete charge-filtered dg Lie superalgebras.

Theorem 115.2 (Hall–Borcherds amalgamation). The completed amalgam exists and represents pairs
of continuous Lie morphisms from 𝔟 𝑋 and 𝔟Δ that agree on 𝔥Π . The Cartan projections of the two Borels
induce canonical retractions

                               𝑟 𝑋 : 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ −→ 𝔟 𝑋 ,         𝑟Δ : 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ −→ 𝔟Δ .
Its completed enveloping algebra is the amalgamated free product
                                     b (𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ )  𝑈
                                     𝑈             b (𝔟 𝑋 )b
                                                           ∗𝑈b (𝔥Π ) 𝑈
                                                                     b (𝔟Δ ) .

The geometric Hall operations survive under 𝑟 𝑋 , while the Igusa bracket, PBW character, and automorphic
determinant survive under 𝑟Δ .
Proof. Construct the ordinary Lie coproduct from the free product of the two Borels and divide by the ideal
identifying their two Cartan copies. Complete by total positive charge and proper root height. Every fixed filtered
degree uses finitely many Lie words, so the completion is separated and has the stated mapping property. The
projection 𝔟 𝑋 → 𝔥Π → 𝔟Δ together with the identity on 𝔟Δ gives 𝑟Δ by universality; the second retraction
is symmetric. The enveloping functor preserves coproducts of Lie algebras, and PBW identifies the completed
algebraic filtration with the displayed completed free product.

Definition 115.3 (Comparison ideal). A primitive comparison 𝜑 : 𝔫Δ5 ,+ → 𝔭+𝑋 preserving Cartan
degree defines the closed ideal

                             J𝜑 = ⟨ 𝑖Δ ( 𝑥 ) − 𝑖 𝑋 ( 𝜑 ( 𝑥 )) | 𝑥 ∈ 𝔫Δ5 ,+ ⟩ ⊂ 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ .


                                                                                                               295
115. The integral Hall–Borcherds amalgam                                                                       296


The diagonal realization is
                                             𝔟 𝑋 ≃Δ,𝜑 = 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ /J𝜑 .

Theorem 115.4 (Universal comparison quotient). A Cartan-preserving primitive comparison is equiv-
alent to a quotient of the integral amalgam that identiﬁes the Borcherds generators with geometric BPS
primitives. If 𝜑 is an isomorphism, the quotient is canonically isomorphic to both Borels. In every case, the
kernel J𝜑 measures the complete discrepancy between the geometric and automorphic primitive theories.
PBW carries the quotient to enveloping algebras, Chevalley chains, current envelopes, and Fock characters.
Proof. A quotient identifying 𝑖Δ ( 𝑥 ) with an element of 𝔭+𝑋 defines 𝜑 , and the Lie relations in the quotient make
it a Lie morphism. Conversely, a Lie morphism makes the generators of J𝜑 compatible with every bracket, so
the quotient exists. An isomorphism 𝜑 gives mutually inverse maps through the two retractions. Functoriality of
enveloping algebras, Chevalley chains, currents, and symmetric-superalgebra completion gives the final statement.


   This amalgam is the maximal theory that retains both sources before a duality identification is imposed.
The geometric Hall source and the automorphic Borcherds source are its two canonical retracts. A
microscopic duality is a diagonal quotient rather than an equality inferred from a scalar character.

                    Coproducts, pairings, and doubles
A positive Hall algebra becomes a quantum group after additional structure is supplied. Let 𝐻 + be a
complete graded bialgebra, let 𝐻 0 be a Cartan algebra, and let 𝐻 − be the opposite or dual half. Let

                                          ⟨−, −⟩ : 𝐻 + ⊗
                                                       b 𝐻 − −→ 𝕜

be a nondegenerate Hopf pairing.

Definition 116.1 (Completed Hall double). The completed Drinfeld double is the topological algebra

                                                       ⊗ 𝐻0 b
                                        b ( 𝐻 +) = 𝐻 − b
                                        𝐷                   ⊗ 𝐻+

with internal products in the three factors and cross-relations determined by the Hopf pairing. Its
representation category is monoidal by the coproduct and braided by the universal 𝑅-matrix whenever
the canonical tensor converges.

Theorem 116.2 (Double construction). Let 𝐻 + and 𝐻 − be paired complete graded bialgebras, let 𝐻 0
be a Cartan bialgebra acting by the stated weight grading, and let

                                          ⟨−, −⟩ : 𝐻 + ⊗
                                                       b 𝐻 − −→ 𝕜

be a continuous nondegenerate Hopf pairing. Assume the triangular multiplication map from 𝐻 − b       ⊗
𝐻 b0      +
     ⊗ 𝐻 is separated and complete. Then the pairing cross-relations determine a unique completed double
with this triangular decomposition. On every continuous module category on which the canonical tensors
converge, the completed canonical element gives the universal 𝑅-matrix.
Proof. The Hopf pairing defines the cross-relations between the paired halves and makes the three canonical em-
beddings bialgebra morphisms. The Hopf identities identify the two reductions of every triple product, proving
associativity. Separated triangular decomposition gives uniqueness. Finite-degree canonical tensors form a compat-
ible inverse system; convergence on the selected module category yields the quasitriangular element [29, 32].

Theorem 116.3 (Quantum Hall–Borcherds amalgam). Let 𝐻𝑋 and 𝐻Δ be complete Hopf algebras
containing the same complete Cartan Hopf algebra 𝐻 0 , with continuous Hopf retractions onto 𝐻 0 . Their
completed amalgamated free product

                                                        ∗ 𝐻 0 𝐻Δ
                                             𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝐻𝑋 b



                                                                                                             297
116. Coproducts, pairings, and doubles                                                                       298


has a unique complete Hopf structure extending the two coproducts, counits, and antipodes. It represents
pairs of continuous Hopf morphisms agreeing on 𝐻 0 and has canonical Hopf retractions to both factors. A
mixed universal 𝑅-matrix is equivalent to a continuous cross-pairing satisfying the two hexagon identities;
the intrinsic 𝑅-matrices of the two retracts remain deﬁned independently.
Proof. The algebraic amalgamated free product exists because complete filtered algebras are obtained degreewise
from finite quotients. The two coproducts agree on 𝐻 0 , so the universal property extends them to a coproduct on
the free product. Coassociativity is checked on the two generating Hopf subalgebras. The same argument extends
the counit and antipode. The Hopf retractions are induced by the factor retractions onto 𝐻 0 . A quasitriangular
structure extending both factors requires the additional commutation data between the two generating halves;
writing this data as a cross-pairing turns the quasitriangular and Yang–Baxter equations into the two hexagon
identities.

    The deconcatenation coproduct on a bar complex and the representation-theoretic coproduct on a
quantum group occupy different structures. A geometric comparison between them is a theorem about
a selected model.

                                           Ordered chiral bialgebras
Let 𝐴 be an ordered chiral algebra on a curve 𝐶 . An ordered chiral bialgebra consists of a coproduct

                                            Δ : 𝐴 −→ 𝐴 b
                                                       ⊗𝐴

that is a morphism of ordered chiral algebras, together with counit and the chosen completion. An
antipode upgrades it to a Hopf object. A spectral coproduct may take values in Laurent series in a
difference parameter.

Theorem 117.1 (Representation fusion). A completed coproduct deﬁnes a tensor product of continuous
𝐴-modules by restriction of scalars. A universal 𝑅-matrix satisfying quasitriangularity deﬁnes a braid
operator on that module category.
Proof. For modules 𝑀 , 𝑁 , pull back the 𝐴 ⊗ b 𝐴 action on 𝑀 ⊗ b 𝑁 along Δ. Coassociativity supplies the asso-
ciator. The relation 𝑅Δ ( 𝑎) = Δop ( 𝑎) 𝑅 makes the flip composed with 𝑅 an intertwiner, and the Yang–Baxter
equation gives the braid relation.

For the quiver 1 → 2 over 𝔽 𝑞 , the twisted Hall algebra has generators 𝐸1 , 𝐸2 and 𝑣 2 = 𝑞 . Counting
subrepresentations gives
                                𝐸12 𝐸2 − ( 𝑣 + 𝑣 −1 ) 𝐸1 𝐸2 𝐸1 + 𝐸2 𝐸12 = 0.
For a representation of dimension vector (2, 1), the arrow has rank zero or one. The relevant flag counts
are
                                      rank 112         121    211

                                                                                                           303
                                                                                              CHAPTER
                                                                                                                        119
                         The tripled Jordan quiver and ℂ3
Let 𝑄 have one vertex and loops 𝑥, 𝑦, 𝑧 , with potential

                                                𝑊 = Tr 𝑥 [ 𝑦, 𝑧 ] .

The Jacobi algebra is ℂ[ 𝑥, 𝑦, 𝑧 ]. The critical CoHA uses the vanishing cycles of Tr 𝑊 on the represen-
tation spaces. Its positive currents are generated by the one-vertex dimension sectors.

Theorem 119.1 (Affine-Yangian positive half). After the standard equivariant localization and completion,
the critical CoHA of the tripled Jordan quiver is identiﬁed with the positive half of the aﬃne Yangian of
𝔤𝔩 1 . The independently deﬁned aﬃne Yangian carries its standard Cartan and negative current halves;
the positive-half comparison embeds the CoHA into that triangular presentation.
Proof. The CoHA product becomes the shuffle product under localization. Dimensional reduction identifies the
tripled-quiver critical theory with the equivariant surface CoHA, and Davison’s affine-BPS theorem identifies its
completed positive algebra with the strictly positive affine Yangian. The shuffle kernel satisfies the affine-Yangian
current relations with equivariant parameters 𝜖1 , 𝜖2 , 𝜖3 and Calabi–Yau relation 𝜖1 + 𝜖2 + 𝜖3 = 0. PBW and current
comparison identify the positive algebra. The independently presented full affine Yangian is the triangular algebra
generated by its negative currents, Cartan currents, and this positive half [22, 24, 71, 72].

   The vacuum representation produces a 𝑊1+∞ -type vertex algebra through an evaluation homomor-
phism. The CoHA, its double, and the vacuum algebra occupy successive levels: positive algebra, paired
quantum group, and distinguished representation.

                                                                                                             120
                  Surface CoHAs and ADE Yangians
Let 𝑆Γ → ℂ2 /Γ be the minimal resolution for a finite subgroup Γ ⊂ SL2 (ℂ). The exceptional curves
have ADE intersection matrix. Moduli of sheaves supported on these curves carry convolution corre-
spondences.

Theorem 120.1 (Curve-supported surface CoHA). The completed positive CoHA of one-dimensional
sheaves supported on the exceptional curves is identiﬁed with a nonstandard positive half of the aﬃne
Yangian of the corresponding ADE Lie algebra.
Proof. The theorem is the curve-supported surface construction of Diaconescu–Porta–Sala–Schiffmann–
Vasserot [26]. The simple curve sheaves give the simple generators, Hecke correspondences give the current
operators, and the completed PBW comparison gives the affine Yangian presentation.

   This theorem is a local K3 model whenever an ADE configuration occurs on a K3 surface. The global
K3 surface adds compact curve classes and monodromy beyond the local root lattice.

                                                                                 CHAPTER
                                                                                                        121
    Coulomb branches and shifted Yangians
For a three-dimensional N = 4 gauge theory, the BFN Coulomb branch is defined by equivariant
Borel–Moore homology of an affine-Grassmannian correspondence. Its convolution algebra quantizes
the Coulomb branch [12]. In quiver gauge theories, shifted Yangians and their truncations provide
presentations of these quantizations. Boundary conditions select the shift and truncation data.
    The BFN construction supplies a second geometric source of Yangians. Its grading and coproduct
arise from loop rotation and convolution, while the surface CoHA source arises from sheaf extensions.
A comparison between the two is a model-specific equivalence.

For a K3 surface 𝑆 , the Mukai lattice is

                            e ( 𝑆, ℤ) = 𝐻 0 ( 𝑆, ℤ) ⊕ 𝐻 2 ( 𝑆, ℤ) ⊕ 𝐻 4 ( 𝑆, ℤ)

                                (( 𝑟, 𝑐, 𝑠 ) , ( 𝑟 ′ , 𝑐 ′ , 𝑠 ′ )) = 𝑐 · 𝑐 ′ − 𝑟 𝑠 ′ − 𝑟 ′ 𝑠.
It is even unimodular of signature (4, 20) and rank 24.

                     The Nakajima Heisenberg algebra
Let                                                M

Theorem 123.1 (K3 Heisenberg action). The operators satisfy
                                                            ∫
                          [ 𝑝𝑚 ( 𝛼 ) , 𝑝𝑛 ( 𝛽 )] = 𝑚 𝛿𝑚+𝑛,0   𝛼 ∪ 𝛽 id .

They generate the rank-twenty-four Heisenberg algebra acting irreducibly on ℍ𝑆 with vacuum
1 ∈ 𝐻 ∗ ( 𝑆 [0] ).
Proof. The commutator is the excess intersection of the two incidence correspondences. Opposite∫creation and

   This action is global, compact, and abelian at the Lie level. It is an exact K3 symmetry and supplies

                                    The orthogonal LLV algebra
For a compact hyperkähler manifold, every Lefschetz class gives an 𝔰𝔩 2 triple on cohomology. The Lie
algebra generated by all such triples is the Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky algebra.

Theorem 124.1 (Orthogonal LLV symmetries). For a K3 surface 𝑆 , the LLV algebra acting on 𝐻 ∗ ( 𝑆 ) is

                                              e ( 𝑆 ) , (−, −)Muk  𝔰𝔬(4, 20) .
                                                                 
                              𝔤LLV ( 𝑆 )  𝔰𝔬 𝐻

For an irreducible hyperkähler manifold 𝑀 of 𝐾 3 [ 𝑛 ] type with 𝑛 ≥ 2, one has 𝑏2 ( 𝑀 ) = 23 and

                                            𝔤LLV ( 𝑀 )  𝔰𝔬(4, 21) .

Moduli spaces of stable objects on 𝑆 carry their own LLV actions; Mukai and monodromy correspondences
relate these actions to the K3 lattice.
Proof. The Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky theorem identifies the Lie algebra generated by all Lefschetz triples with
the orthogonal algebra of the Mukai extension 𝐻 2 ( 𝑀 ) ⊕ 𝑈 . For 𝑆 , the extension has signature (4, 20). For
𝐾 3 [ 𝑛 ] type with 𝑛 ≥ 2, the Beauville–Bogomolov lattice has signature (3, 20), so adjoining the hyperbolic plane
gives signature (4, 21) [58, 73].

   The LLV algebra is a classical cohomological symmetry. A Yangian deformation of the full orthogonal
action consists of two further structures: a Drinfeld current presentation and a compatible spectral
coproduct.




                                                                                                               317
                                                                                CHAPTER
                                                                                                       125
                                                   Local ADE Yangians
An ADE configuration of (−2)-curves on 𝑆 gives a root sublattice of 𝐻 2 ( 𝑆, ℤ). Sheaves supported
on the configuration produce the surface CoHA of the preceding part and hence a completed positive
affine ADE Yangian. Nakajima quiver varieties supply compatible Yangian actions on their equivariant
cohomology.
    Thus K3 geometry contains local nonabelian Yangian sectors indexed by ADE enhancements of the
Picard lattice. Picard monodromy transports the ADE root sublattices over the corresponding Noether–
Lefschetz loci. A family of sheaf moduli and Hecke correspondences compatible with that monodromy
transports the Yangian actions themselves.

                                 The K3 symmetry trichotomy
The three exact structures are

  Structure                  Carrier                                           Operation
  Mukai Heisenberg                 ∗
                              𝑛 𝐻 (𝑆

A global K3 quantum algebra is a system that contains these three actions with explicit comparison

                         The K3 correspondence category
Let 𝑆 be a projective K3 surface. Let M 𝑆 contain the smooth projective moduli spaces 𝑀𝜎 ( 𝑣 ) of stable
objects for primitive Mukai vectors and generic stability conditions, the Hilbert schemes 𝑆 [ 𝑛 ] , and
the equivariant curve-supported moduli at every ADE enhancement. Close this collection under finite

Definition 127.1 (K3 correspondence category). The category Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) has the spaces of M 𝑆 as
objects and
                               Hom( 𝑀 , 𝑁 ) = 𝐻∗BM ( 𝑀 × 𝑁 )
for proper objects, with the corresponding localized equivariant Borel–Moore theory on ADE charts.
Composition is convolution:                                    

The tensor product is Cartesian product of moduli spaces and external product of correspondences.

Theorem 127.2 (Associativity and monoidality). Transverse convolution makes Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) a graded
monoidal category. The cohomology functor

                                            𝜔𝑆 ( 𝑀 ) = 𝐻 ∗ ( 𝑀 )

and its localized equivariant form on ADE charts is strong monoidal and carries convolution to composition
of linear operators.
Proof. The two parenthesizations of three correspondences are the two push–pull descriptions on 𝑀1 × 𝑀2 ×
𝑀3 × 𝑀4 . Proper base change and the projection formula identify them. Cartesian products and external products
satisfy the interchange law. The standard action of a kernel on cohomology is compatible with both operations.

   Nakajima incidence varieties are morphisms in this category. Cup product and Lefschetz adjoints are
diagonal kernels. ADE Hecke correspondences are equivariant morphisms. Thus the three K3 symmetry
families occupy one correspondence category before passage to any enveloping algebra.

Theorem 128.2 (Exact K3 symmetry envelope). The algebra A 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) is separated and complete. It

   The three symmetries retain their intrinsic forms inside one algebra. Their interaction is calculated
by intersections of the corresponding cycles. On a Noether–Lefschetz locus, an LLV operator and an
ADE Hecke operator meet in the same correspondence space; the resulting convolution is the geometric
cross-relation.

Theorem 129.2 (K3 Tannaka theorem). On every ﬁnite Mukai-norm truncation, C 𝑆 is equivalent to
the rigid exact tensor subcategory of ﬁnite comodules over O (𝔾 𝐾 3 ) generated by the universal coactions on
the ﬁbres 𝜔 𝑆 ( 𝑀 ). Every geometric K3 correspondence induces a comodule morphism. The continuous dual

ﬁbre functor. A braided transport on C 𝑆 produces a coquasitriangular structure on the coend.
Proof. Exact rigid coend reconstruction applies to the faithful exact strong monoidal functor 𝜔 𝑆 . The coend carries
the universal coaction on each fibre. The exact tensor envelope used to define C 𝑆 is precisely the subcategory
generated from these comodules by the operations preserved by 𝜔 𝑆 , which gives the stated equivalence. The
quotient map from geometric correspondences makes each correspondence a comodule morphism. Dualizing the
universal coaction represents tensor-compatible natural endomorphisms. A braiding supplies the bilinear form
whose hexagon identities are the coquasitriangular axioms.

   The action algebra A 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) and the Tannaka algebra 𝑈 (𝔾 𝐾 3 ) are complementary. The first is gen-
erated by geometric kernels. The second consists of tensor symmetries compatible with every kernel.
Their commutants record two complementary aspects of the K3 correspondence geometry: geometric
operators and tensor-compatible natural symmetries.

                                                                                    CHAPTER
                                                                                                            134
                                                             Physical meaning
The protected avatar is the complete universal algebraic model selected by the Igusa root datum. The
Ginzburg category supplies its noncommutative three-Calabi–Yau sector. The quiver Hall category
supplies its ordinary extension geometry. The Borcherds root superspace supplies its protected one-
particle states. The colored Ran object supplies its local and wrapped current algebra on the elliptic
curve. Hong’s quantum Borcherds algebra supplies its line-operator symmetry. The root Dirac family
supplies its first-order determinant, and the Fock construction supplies the scalar square.
   A compact string construction with specified simple-root states and extension amplitudes maps to
the protected avatar through the presentation universal property. The comparison is determined on
primitives; PBW propagates it to multiparticle states, the Hall product, the current algebra, the quantum
double, and the determinant.




                                                                                                     339
                                                                                        CHAPTER
                                                                                                                135
Universal property of the protected avatar
A presentation-preserving protected theory consists of a three-Calabi–Yau category, an oriented Hall
boundary, a colored elliptic factorization algebra, a quasitriangular quantum symmetry, an oriented
determinant, and comparison functors from the Borcherds generators-and-relations construction to
these five objects. The chosen simple-root states satisfy the Gritsenko–Nikulin Cartan, parity, and Serre
relations.

Theorem 135.1 (Presentation universal property). Every presentation-preserving protected theory carries
a unique primitive Lie-superalgebra morphism

                                           𝔫Δ5 ,+ −→ Prim(𝔗)

extending the chosen simple-root states. The Hall, Ginzburg, current, quantum-group, and determinant
functors extend this primitive morphism to the ﬁve selected comparison maps. A primitive isomorphism
together with equivalences of the selected functorial realizations gives an equivalence of protected data.
Proof. The Borcherds generators and relations give the unique primitive morphism. Each remaining construction
is functorial in a presentation map: quiver Hall convolution in the quiver map, Calabi–Yau completion in the
dg-algebra map, chiral Chevalley theory in the Lie map, Hong quantization in the Cartan superdatum, and the
diagonal Fredholm operator in the root-graded map. PBW promotes the primitive isomorphism to the completed
enveloping and Fock objects.

Proof. A full inclusion of root data adds vertices and arrows to the Borcherds quiver. Keller completion and

 From positive half to full quantum group
Suppose the compact or local positive algebra 𝐻 + is paired continuously with a negative half 𝐻 − , carries
a completed coproduct, and admits Cartan data and a separated triangular completion. The completed
double
                                             U=𝐷   b ( 𝐻 +)

has triangular decomposition 𝐻 − b   ⊗ 𝐻0 ⊗b 𝐻 + . Its universal 𝑅-matrix acts on continuous tensor prod-

coproduct asymmetry.
   For ℂ3 , 𝐻 + is the positive affine Yangian. For local ADE surfaces, it is the completed positive affine

                                               Tannaka reconstruction
Let L be an essentially small rigid 𝕜-linear abelian monoidal category with finite-dimensional morphism
spaces, finite-length objects, and exact tensor product. Let

                                             𝜔 : L → Vectfd

be a faithful exact strong monoidal functor. Assume the coend
                                           ∫ 𝑀∈L
                                     𝐻=             𝜔 ( 𝑀 )∗ ⊗ 𝜔 ( 𝑀 )

exists and that the canonical comparison functor to finite-dimensional 𝐻 -comodules is comonadic. Ten-
sor product makes 𝐻 a bialgebra, and rigidity supplies its antipode. A braiding gives a coquasitriangular
form; a meromorphic braiding gives its spectral version.

Theorem 138.1 (Rigid Tannaka reconstruction). Under the preceding ﬁnite and comonadic hypotheses,
the comparison functor is a monoidal equivalence

                                           L ≃ Comodfd ( 𝐻 ) .

Braiding transports to the universal coquasitriangular form on 𝐻 .
Proof. Matrix coefficients define the universal 𝐻 -coaction on every 𝜔 ( 𝑀 ). Tensor product and duality give
the bialgebra and antipode structures. The Barr–Beck comonadic theorem identifies L with coalgebras for the
induced comonad, and the coend identifies that comonad with 𝐻 ⊗ −. The hexagon identities transport to the
coquasitriangular identities.

   This reconstruction explains why a line-operator category, a fiber functor, and exchange together
produce a quantum group. The ordered boundary algebra alone provides fusion; the plane of exchange
supplies braiding.

                                                                                                    351
                                                                                      CHAPTER

                                        The centre and the double
The Hochschild centre 𝑍 ord ( 𝐴) is the universal E2 algebra acting centrally on an ordered algebra. The
Drinfeld centre 𝑍 (Mod 𝐴 ) is the braided category of modules equipped with half-braidings. Under
dualizability hypotheses these are related by the passage from algebraic Hochschild cochains to categorical
endomorphisms of the identity. Derived loop-space descriptions of centres provide the geometric form
of this relation [7].
    The centre and the double answer complementary questions. The centre classifies central bulk actions.
The double reconstructs a full quantum group from paired positive and negative halves. A field theory
can supply a comparison by realizing both as line and bulk operator constructions.

Theorem 141.1 (Classical compact-background source). The action (141) satisﬁes the classical master

                                                                                                                   357
                                                                                               CHAPTER
                                                                                                                         142
       Tree exactness and the quantum master
                                    equation
Every interaction vertex in (141) has one 𝐵 -leg and two 𝐴-legs. The propagator pairs an 𝐴-leg with a
𝐵 -leg.

Lemma 142.1 (Loop bound). A connected Feynman graph of the cotangent theory has loop number at
most one.
Proof. A graph with 𝑉 interaction vertices has exactly 𝑉 internal or external 𝐵 -half-edges. Every internal edge
consumes one of them, hence 𝐸 ≤ 𝑉 . The loop number is 𝐸 − 𝑉 + 1 ≤ 1.

   The positive algebra is graded by strictly positive root height. For homogeneous 𝑥 ∈ 𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) , the
operator ad 𝑥 raises height. Its coadjoint operator lowers dual height. A product of adjoint operators
labelled by positive roots has total nonzero height shift and therefore has zero trace.

Theorem 142.2 (Perturbative exactness). Every one-loop Lie weight of the ﬁnite-height theory vanishes.
The renormalized quantum master equation holds with zero counterterm, and the perturbative effective
action is represented by tree graphs. The statement is compatible with the height maps 𝐻 → 𝐻 + 1.
Proof. The loop bound leaves tree graphs and one-loop graphs. Their Lie factor is a cyclic trace of a product of
adjoint or coadjoint operators. Positive height makes each such product strictly triangular, so its trace is zero. The
anomaly and the one-loop effective vertex therefore vanish. Tree graphs satisfy the classical master equation by the
usual boundary-stratum argument. Every map preserves root height.

Corollary 142.3 (Height-complete quantum source). The coeﬃcientwise inverse limit

                                              ObsΔ𝑋 = lim Obs 𝑋( 𝐻 )
                                                      ←−
                                                          𝐻

is a perturbative quantum factorization algebra on 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 . Its primitive gauge algebra is the complete
positive Igusa Borcherds algebra.
Proof. Every fixed root height stabilizes in the finite tower. The factorization maps and quantum differential are
compatible with the truncations, so the separated inverse limit preserves the quantum master equation and the
primitive Lie algebra.

The Dolbeault algebra of a K3 surface has the strict harmonic model

                                𝐻 0,• ( 𝑆, O𝑆 ) = ℂ1 ⊕ ℂ 𝜎¯𝑆 [−2] ,        𝜎¯𝑆2 = 0.

The trace pairs 1 with 𝜎¯𝑆 .

Theorem 143.1 (K3 harmonic reduction). Factorization pushforward along 𝑝 𝐸 : 𝑆 × 𝐸 → 𝐸 , followed
by the K3 harmonic deformation retract, gives a quasi-isomorphism

                𝑝 𝐸,∗ Obs 𝑋( 𝐻 ) ≃ Obs 𝐸 𝑇 ∗ [−1] Cur 𝐸 (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ) ⊗ 𝐻 0,• ( 𝑆, O𝑆 ) .

The primitive degree-zero current sector is Cur 𝐸 (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ). The degree-two K3 class is its cotangent partner.
Proof. Kähler Hodge theory gives a deformation retract of the Dolbeault complex onto harmonic forms. The
harmonic representatives 1 and 𝜎¯𝑆 are closed under multiplication, so the retract is a cyclic dg-algebra quasi-
isomorphism. Tensoring the local cyclic Lie model with this retract and applying homological transfer gives the
displayed cotangent current theory. The product 𝜎¯𝑆2 vanishes by complex dimension, so higher transferred products
vanish.

Corollary 143.2 (Exact curve shadow). The K3 harmonic reduction of the compact six-dimensional source
is the cotangent current factorization algebra of the Igusa positive root algebra on 𝐸 . On a formal disc, its

polarization of Volume II.
Proof. The harmonic reduction theorem identifies the finite-height curve theories. Chiral PBW identifies the
associative chiral bar of the current envelope with chiral Chevalley chains. Proper height passes this statement to
the complete limit. Boundary polarization of the cotangent theory gives the transverse enveloping algebra on a
collar.

   This construction gives a compact effective field-theoretic source whose gauge algebra is selected by
the Igusa root datum. The geometric 𝐸 -equivariant Hall algebra constructed above gives an independent
compact D-brane source. A primitive root framing compares its extension algebra with the effective
source.

                         The compact-source comparison
Let 𝔓Δ ( 𝑋 ) denote the compact cotangent theory above, its K3 harmonic shadow, its Hall boundary
category from the Borcherds quiver, and its root Dirac determinant.

Theorem 145.1 (Compact protected source theorem). The system 𝔓Δ ( 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 ) contains, in one functo-
rial construction:
   (i) a quantum factorization algebra on the compact Calabi–Yau threefold;
  (ii) a curve-level local–wrapped current algebra on 𝐸 ;
 (iii) an ordinary quiver Hall boundary, a parity-reﬁned root superspace, and a three-Calabi–Yau
       Ginzburg completion;
 (iv) Hong’s height-complete quantized Borcherds superalgebra;
  (v) the Fredholm Pfaﬃan DenΔ and the scalar trace −( 𝚥∗ DenΔ ) −2 = − 𝜒10−1 .
Every arrow among these objects preserves root degree, parity, product, and completion.
Proof. The compact BV theory and its quantum exactness were proved above. Harmonic reduction gives the
curve theory. The Borcherds quiver and Ginzburg completion give the Hall boundary. Hong’s construction gives
the quantum group. The root Hamiltonian gives the determinant. Each construction is functorial in finite root
windows, and proper height gives the common limit.

   The theorem realizes the complete universal root-datum protected model over the 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 back-
ground. The geometric D-brane theory enters through a monoidal functor from its oriented extension
category to the Hall boundary of 𝔓Δ . Such a functor is determined by primitive branes and their exten-
sion amplitudes.

A three-dimensional holomorphic–topological theory on 𝐶 × ℝ has local observables that are holomor-

                                            Bar(𝑈 (𝔩)) ≃ 𝐶∗ (𝔩) .

                        Six-dimensional hCS on 𝐾 3 × 𝐸

                                           Ω0,∗ ( 𝑆 × 𝐸, 𝔞) [1]

and action 𝑆hCS . Its factorization observables are native to the threefold. Pushforward along 𝑝 𝐸 gives a

                                               Scalarization hierarchy
The constructions descend through four levels:

            factorization or Hall object −→ primitive Lie algebra
                                        −→ PBW character −→ scalar partition function.

Each arrow retains the quotient of structure displayed at its target. The first object contains supports,
extension maps, and brackets. The second retains the infinitesimal algebra. The third retains signed
graded dimensions. The fourth adds a trace and normalization. Upward reconstruction is supplied by
the corresponding geometric operations and comparison morphisms.

                                         The foundational theorem
Definition 154.1 (Complete Calabi–Yau quantum-group datum). A complete datum is a governing
tuple 𝔇 together with:
  (i) PTVV-representable moduli for the smooth proper Calabi–Yau category;
  (ii) a renormalized BV quantization satisfying the quantum master equation and a local compact
       factorization generator 𝑏 ;
 (iii) a boundary reduction or proper locally trivial map to a curve satisfying factorization pushforward,
       together with the supplied curve-level faces and aligned distributive law;
 (iv) a strong-oriented Hall coefficient theory whose pull–push maps satisfy base change and whose
      positive algebra is separated and complete;
  (v) paired negative and Cartan halves, a completed coproduct, a continuous nondegenerate Hopf
      pairing, and a separated triangular completion on which the canonical tensors converge;
 (vi) compatible open–closed and Hall–boundary morphisms;
 (vii) a parity-refined primitive comparison for every automorphic identification.

Theorem 154.2 (Calabi–Yau quantum-group assembly). Let a complete datum consist of:
  (i) a Calabi–Yau category and a representable moduli-of-objects problem;
  (ii) a quantized cyclic elliptic BV theory and a boundary or defect generator;
 (iii) a Hall extension correspondence with coeﬃcient functor, base-change theorem, strong orientation
       data, and separated charge completion;
 (iv) paired positive and negative bialgebras, Cartan data, a nondegenerate continuous Hopf pairing,
      and a completed double;
  (v) every curve-level distributive, PBW, and analytic comparison used below.
Then the datum determines functorially:
  (i) framed E2 Hochschild cochains and the shifted-symplectic moduli structure;
  (ii) the three curve-level algebraic faces and their three bars;
 (iii) the positive Hall/BPS algebra and its primitive Lie algebra;
 (iv) the completed quantum double and braided representation category;




                                                                                                      389
154. The foundational theorem                                                                                   390


  (v) every supplied comparison among categorical, ﬁeld-theoretic, Hall, current, quantum, and automor-
      phic realizations;
  (vi) the Weyl-shifted PBW/Chevalley character of any parity-reﬁned primitive Lie-superalgebra identi-
       ﬁcation.
Proof. Cyclic Deligne and shifted symplectic geometry give the first output [10, 11, 66]. Boundary factorization
gives the curve faces, and Volume I gives their bars and centres. The Hall correspondence and its base-change
theorem give the associative product; strong orientation supplies the coefficient coherence required in the 3-Calabi–
Yau setting [52]. The paired bialgebra data give the double and universal 𝑅-matrix. The comparison maps commute
by hypothesis. PBW and Chevalley antisymmetrization give the final character. Each construction is natural under
a morphism preserving the displayed data.

   The local quiver, equivariant surface, K3 correspondence, and Igusa Borcherds chapters construct
objects in their stated categories. A geometric 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 Hall theory determines a morphism from its
primitive and determinant-line data to the universal Igusa objects.

     Closure theorem for the established and
                             universal layers
Theorem 156.1 (Foundational closure). The following layers are complete mathematical constructions:
   (i) framed Hochschild and shifted-symplectic categorical data;
  (ii) perturbative factorization observables from a quantized cyclic elliptic BV theory;
 (iii) the three curve-level algebraic faces and their bars;
 (iv) Hall products in the established quiver, surface, 2-Calabi–Yau, and strong-oriented 3-Calabi–Yau
      settings;
  (v) the K3 Heisenberg, LLV, and local ADE Yangian symmetry atlas;
 (vi) the Igusa Borcherds root algebra, its quantized Borcherds superalgebra, proper-height bar/current
      system, classical local BF theory, exact ﬁnite zero-mode BV theory, and analytic determinant;
 (vii) the primitive reduced 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 scalar character.
A compact 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 quantum-group realization is exactly an instance of the compact comparison theorem
with a constructed reduced Hall datum.
Proof. Items (1)–(7) are the theorems cited and proved in the corresponding chapters. The last sentence is the
definition of a compact realization together with the preceding recognition theorem.

                                                                                                   395
                                                                                       CHAPTER
                                                                                                               158

                            Concordance with the original
                                     Calabi–Yau volume
The reconstitution preserves the original vision of a bridge among Calabi–Yau categories, holomorphic
field theory, chiral algebras, Hall algebras, quantum groups, and automorphic products. It realizes that
vision through the following replacements:
   (i) framed E2 Hochschild cochains and (2 − 𝑑 )-shifted symplectic moduli replace a dimension-
       dependent Hochschild E𝑛 assertion;
  (ii) a geometric BV theory and boundary category produce factorization observables and transverse
       ordered chiral algebras; associative chiral convolution is recorded as a separate supplied structure;
 (iii) pushforward along an actual map 𝑝 : 𝑋 → 𝐶 replaces formal cycle-specialization notation;
 (iv) extension stacks, coefficient sheaves, orientations, and completions define the Hall algebra;
  (v) coproduct, Cartan, Hopf pairing, and topology define the double;
 (vi) K3 Heisenberg, LLV orthogonal symmetry, local ADE Yangians, and global BPS generalized Kac–
      Moody algebras occupy distinct strata joined by explicit comparison maps where constructed;
 (vii) the graph of the 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 Gram map becomes multiplicative in the polarized central extension;
(viii) point-local and wrapped sectors form a hybrid factorization problem;
 (ix) the Igusa form is the Weyl-shifted PBW/Chevalley character of 𝔤Δ5 , and compact Hall geometry
      maps to it through the primitive comparison;
  (x) anomaly identities are transported through explicit maps between deformation complexes.
These constructions retain the scale of the original program while placing every operation in its natural
category.

                                                 The three K3 brackets
The Heisenberg bracket is the excess intersection of two Nakajima incidence kernels. The LLV bracket
is the commutator of diagonal Lefschetz kernels and their adjoints. The local Yangian bracket is the
convolution commutator of ADE Hecke kernels with spectral equivariance. In Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) all three are
compositions of geometric kernels. Their mixed commutators are the corresponding triple-intersection
classes. The action algebra and Tannaka coend retain these classes while preserving the three geometric
constructions.

  (i) the low Fourier rows of 𝜙0,1 through 𝑞 3 by exact theta-series division;
  (ii) the vector-valued cocycle identity for the Gram polarization;
 (iii) the rational Yang–Baxter equation on (ℂ2 ) ⊗3 ;
 (iv) the 𝐴2 Hall flag counts at ranks zero and one;
  (v) the type-II Gram matrix and reflection identities.
Each calculation uses integer or rational arithmetic and appears as a worked formula in the relevant
chapter.

Theorem 162.3 (Four-colour differential). The interface differential squares to zero. Its codimension-
two terms are exactly the associative relations in the two colours, the two module relations, and the mixed
distributive relation.

162. Three products and one interface                                                                          412


Proof. Filter by total word length. Every component of the square contracts two adjacent edges. Disjoint contrac-
tions cancel by the determinant-line sign. Nested contractions in one colour cancel by associativity. A contraction
meeting the interface cancels by the corresponding module axiom. The remaining square has one edge of each
colour and is the aligned distributive square. The datum 𝜆 identifies its two composites.

Compatible modules and the mixed centre
A compatible module is a triple ( 𝑀 ch , 𝑀 ⊥ , 𝜅 ) with an 𝐴ch action, an 𝐴⊥ action, and an interface
comparison over 𝐾 . Let Modmix

Definition 163.1 (Mixed derived centre). The mixed derived centre is
                                                            
                                     𝑍 mix (𝔸) = End idModmix .     𝔸


It is computed by the endomorphisms of the diagonal compatible bimodule.

Theorem 163.2 (Universal interior action). 𝑍 mix (𝔸) carries an internal E2 algebra structure. Every
coherent interior action on the mixed boundary category factors uniquely through this centre. Morita-
equivalent complete mixed data have equivalent centres.
Proof. Endomorphisms of the identity functor form the higher centre of a module category. Composition and
insertion of natural transformations give the brace operations and hence the E2 structure. An interior operator acts
naturally on every compatible module and therefore defines an endomorphism of the identity. The factorization
is the universal property of the endomorphism object. Morita equivalence identifies the module categories and
their identity functors.

     Relative induction from a chiral algebra
Let 𝑉 be a chiral algebra with conformal vacuum. Its algebraic collar Coll(𝑉 ) is the free transverse
E1 extension generated by one boundary copy of 𝑉 , modulo the chiral relations already present in 𝑉 .
Weight completion gives Coll(
                         d 𝑉 ).

Theorem 164.1 (Universal collar). For every complete transversely ordered chiral algebra 𝐵 and every
chiral morphism 𝑉 → 𝐵 , there is a unique continuous E1 morphism

                                                Coll(
                                                d 𝑉 ) −→ 𝐵

extending the chiral map. The construction is left adjoint to restriction from transverse ordered algebras to
chiral coeﬃcients.
Proof. The algebraic collar is the relative operadic induction from the chiral operad to the mixed operad. Relative
induction is left adjoint to restriction. Completion preserves the adjunction on positive complete objects because
every map is determined weight by weight.

Theorem 165.1 (Complete deformation 𝐿∞ algebra). The convolution complex has a canonical complete
𝐿∞ structure. Its Maurer–Cartan points are complete mixed boundary structures together with a coherent
central action. Twisting by the point deﬁned by 𝔸 gives the tangent algebra of deformations of the full
datum.
Proof. The infinitesimal decomposition maps of the bar cooperad insert collections of operations into one an-
other. Composition in the endomorphism operad turns these decompositions into the convolution brackets. The
cooperad identities imply the 𝐿∞ identities. A Maurer–Cartan element is precisely an operad morphism from a
cofibrant resolution of Pmix to End𝔸 . Twisting linearizes this mapping space at the selected structure.

Theorem 168.1 (Graphwise factorization). Renormalized graph weights that extend to the compactiﬁed
conﬁguration spaces and satisfy the quantum master equation deﬁne a factorization algebra of quantum
observables. The boundary restriction of this factorization algebra realizes the original mixed datum 𝔸.
Proof. Disjoint unions of configurations multiply graph weights. Stokes’ theorem on each compactified configu-
ration space expresses the differential of a weight as the sum over boundary contractions. The quantum master
equation cancels the codimension-one contractions. The resulting cochains satisfy Weiss descent. Restriction to a
collar recovers the generating operations used to define the deformation algebra.

                                          The Koszul–Morita square
Let 𝑃 be a dualizable boundary state for 𝐴⊥ . Its boundary dual is

                                             𝐴!𝑃 = RHom 𝐴⊥ ( 𝑃 , 𝑃 ) .

The two-sided bar sends the diagonal bimodule to a diagonal bicomodule.

Theorem 169.1 (Mixed Koszul–Morita transport). On the positive complete locus, the universal twisting
cochains identify complete mixed modules with compatible comodules over the four bar objects. Derived
tensor products of interfaces become derived cotensor products. The mixed centre and mixed Hochschild
chains are preserved.
Proof. Apply one-colour bar–cobar duality to each associative colour. The interface bar is a bimodule bar and
its filtration is finite in each total weight. The aligned distributive law identifies the two orders of transformation.
Eilenberg–Moore comparison sends tensor to cotensor. Endomorphisms of the diagonal and its trace are invariant
under the induced Morita equivalence.

                                                                                            CHAPTER
                                                                                                                     170
                                 Planar exchange and Tannaka
                                               reconstruction
A second topological direction gives paths exchanging parallel defects. Their homotopy classes form
braid groups. A rigid meromorphic line category L with a faithful fibre functor 𝜔 has coend
                                              ∫ 𝑀 ∈L
                                    O (𝔾) =              𝜔 ( 𝑀 )∗ ⊗ 𝜔 ( 𝑀 ).

Theorem 170.1 (Quantum symmetry from exchange). The coend is a coquasitriangular bialgebra. Its
continuous dual acts on every line object. The meromorphic braiding gives the universal 𝑅-matrix on
completed matrix coeﬃcients. A spectral rational braiding yields an RTT presentation.
Proof. Tensor product in L gives the coproduct on matrix coefficients. Evaluation gives the counit. Rigidity gives
the antipode on the Hopf envelope. The braiding defines the coquasitriangular pairing, and the hexagon identities
give the Yang–Baxter equation. Writing this naturality on a generating fibre produces the RTT relation.

                                                                   BRST reduction
Let 𝑄 be a square-zero derivation of a mixed datum. Extend 𝑄 factorwise to every bar word. Since 𝑄 is
a derivation for each structure map, it anticommutes with every bar differential.

Theorem 171.1 (BRST–bar bicomplex). The total differential

                                         𝐷 = 𝑄 + 𝑏ch + 𝑏⊥ + 𝑏mix

satisﬁes 𝐷 2 = 0. A bounded-below complete ﬁltration gives a convergent spectral sequence

                            𝐸1 = 𝐻𝑄 Barmix (𝔸; 𝐾 ) =⇒ 𝐻𝐷 Barmix (𝔸; 𝐾 ) .
                                                                      


When 𝑄 -cohomology is concentrated and tensor-exact, the target is the mixed bar of the reduced datum.
Proof. The square vanishes by the derivation identity and the four-colour bar theorem. The filtration by BRST
degree gives the spectral sequence. Concentration and tensor exactness identify the 𝐸1 differential with the bar
differential of the reduced operations.

                             tangent complex
Let P𝑐 ( 𝑋 ) be the formal moduli problem of projective connections with central parameter 𝑐 . At a
projective connection 𝑝, its tangent complex is locally
                                                      D 𝑝,𝑐
                                    Ω0,• ( 𝑋 , 𝑇𝑋 ) −−−→ Ω0,• ( 𝑋 , 𝐾𝑋⊗2 ) ,

where
                                                                          𝑐 3
                                     D 𝑝,𝑐 ( 𝜖 ) = 𝜖𝜕 𝑝 + 2( 𝜕 𝜖 ) 𝑝 +      𝜕 𝜖.

Theorem 172.1 (Projective covariance). A conformal map Vir𝑐 → 𝐴 deﬁnes a formal family of mixed
structures over P𝑐 ( 𝑋 ). Its tangent algebra is the semidirect product of the Virasoro projective complex with
the deformation algebra relative to a ﬁxed projective connection.
Proof. The conformal vector acts on fields by infinitesimal coordinate change. The central term is the affine

tangent algebra to derivations of the mixed datum. The tangent algebra of the associated family is the semidirect
product.

Three-dimensional gravity as a flat Cartan

                   [ 𝐽𝑎 , 𝐽𝑏 ] = 𝜖 𝑎𝑏 𝑐 𝐽𝑐 ,   [ 𝐽𝑎 , 𝑃𝑏 ] = 𝜖 𝑎𝑏 𝑐 𝑃𝑐 ,   [ 𝑃𝑎 , 𝑃𝑏 ] = ℓ −2 𝜖 𝑎𝑏 𝑐 𝐽𝑐 .

The invariant pairing is ⟨ 𝐽𝑎 , 𝑃𝑏 ⟩ = 𝜂𝑎𝑏 and vanishes on the two diagonal summands. A Cartan connec-
tion is
                                             A = 𝜔 𝑎 𝐽𝑎 + 𝑒 𝑎 𝑃𝑎 .
Its curvature is                                1                 
                                      𝐹A = 𝑅 𝑎 + 2 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 ∧ 𝑒 𝑐 𝐽𝑎 + 𝑇 𝑎 𝑃𝑎 .
                                                2ℓ
Theorem 173.1 (Cartan–Einstein equivalence). On the locus where 𝑒 is invertible, 𝐹A = 0 is equivalent
to
                     𝑇 𝑎 = 0,      𝐺 𝜇𝜈 + Λ 𝑔 𝜇𝜈 = 0,      𝑔 𝜇𝜈 = 𝜂𝑎𝑏 𝑒 𝜇𝑎 𝑒 𝜈𝑏 .
The Chern–Simons action for A equals the Einstein–Hilbert action with cosmological constant, up to its
boundary term and normalization.
Proof. The 𝑃 component of the flatness equation is the torsion equation. Invertibility of 𝑒 gives the Levi–Civita

[1, 74].

                                                                                           CHAPTER
                                                                                                                    174
                         Four equivalent linear complexes
Fix a constant-curvature solution ( 𝑒, 𝜔). There are four deformation complexes.
   (i) The flat adjoint complex Ω• ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) with differential 𝑑 A .
  (ii) The Einstein–Cartan complex on ( 𝛿 𝑒, 𝛿 𝜔) with linearized torsion and curvature.
 (iii) The metric Einstein complex on ℎ 𝜇𝜈 with linearized Einstein operator.
 (iv) The Calabi complex resolving local Killing fields by the Killing, linearized curvature, and Bianchi
      operators.

Theorem 174.1 (Chain equivalence of gravitational presentations). An invertible torsion-free dreibein
gives chain maps among the four complexes. These maps are quasi-isomorphisms on every geodesically
convex open set. Their cohomology describes inﬁnitesimal isometries, physical linearized ﬁelds, and Bianchi
identities in the four languages.
Proof. The dreibein identifies internal translations with tangent vectors and internal Lorentz rotations with
skew endomorphisms. Splitting 𝑑 A in this basis gives the linearized torsion and curvature operators. Eliminating
𝛿 𝜔 by the linearized torsion equation gives the metric Einstein operator. The symbol sequence is the Calabi
symbol sequence and is exact on constant-curvature space. The Poincaré lemma for the flat adjoint connection
and exactness of the symbol homotopies give local quasi-isomorphisms.

      Einstein completion of a conformal pair
Let 𝐴 𝐿 and 𝐴 𝑅 be conformal mixed boundary data with equal Brown–Henneaux central charge. Their
projective families map to the same P𝑐 ( 𝑋 ). Define their relative field algebra by the homotopy fibre
product over the projective tangent algebra.

Definition 176.1 (Einstein BV completion). The Einstein completion is

                         EinBV3 ( 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴 𝑅 ) = 𝑇 ∗ [−1] MC 𝔤grav ⋉Ham 𝔪 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴𝑅 .
                                                                                 


Here 𝔤grav is the Cartan deformation algebra and 𝔪 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴𝑅 is the relative mixed deformation algebra after
projective matching.

Theorem 176.2 (Einstein completion theorem). The completion has a canonical BV action. Its pure
gravitational sector is three-dimensional Einstein gravity. Its matter sector is the relative deformation
theory of the conformal pair. The coupling is the moment map for the projective and diffeomorphism
actions. The Bianchi identity of the full system is the 𝐿∞ identity of the semidirect product.
Proof. The homotopy fibre product matches the projective gauge fields and retains the relative matter directions.
The gravitational algebra acts on those directions by conformal transport. The semidirect product is therefore
an 𝐿∞ algebra. Shifted cotangent transgression gives the BV action and its master equation. Restriction to the
gravitational summand gives the Cartan action. The mixed term is the Hamiltonian pairing of the gauge field with
the matter moment map.

Theorem 177.1 (Principal higher-spin spectrum). The principal embedding gives one massless gauge
ﬁeld of each integer spin 2, 3, . . . , 𝑁 . Classical Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction of the boundary aﬃne Poisson

Einstein–Cartan completion with the same spin spectrum.

                      The four complexes in coordinates
178.1     Flat adjoint complex
At a flat Cartan connection A, the first complex is

                     Ω0 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω1 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω2 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω3 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) .
                                     𝑑A                     𝑑A                      𝑑A


Degree zero is the gauge parameter, degree one is the fluctuation, degree two is the linearized curvature,
and degree three is the Bianchi identity.

178.2     Einstein–Cartan complex
Write a gauge parameter as ( 𝜉 𝑎 , 𝜆 𝑎 ) and a fluctuation as ( 𝑢 𝑎 , 𝑣 𝑎 ) = ( 𝛿 𝑒 𝑎 , 𝛿 𝜔 𝑎 ). The first differential is

                                            𝑢 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝜉 𝑎 + 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 𝜆 𝑐 ,
                                            𝑣 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝜆 𝑎 + ℓ −2 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 𝜉 𝑐 .

The second differential is
                                          𝛿𝑇 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝑢 𝑎 + 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑣 𝑏 ∧ 𝑒 𝑐 ,
                                          𝛿 𝐹 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝑣 𝑎 + ℓ −2 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 ∧ 𝑢 𝑐 .
The third differential is the linearized pair of Cartan Bianchi identities.

Proposition 178.1 (Chain identity). The composite of the ﬁrst two Einstein–Cartan differentials vanishes
on a constant-curvature torsion-free background.
Proof. Apply 𝐷 2𝜔 to 𝜉 and 𝜆. Replace the background curvature by −ℓ −2 𝑒 ∧ 𝑒 and use 𝐷 𝜔 𝑒 = 0. The curvature
terms cancel the explicit ℓ −2 terms. The remaining expressions are Jacobi identities for 𝜖 𝑎𝑏 𝑐 .

Theorem 178.2 (Explicit metric reduction). Solving the linearized torsion equation for 𝑣 = 𝛿 𝜔 and
substituting into the linearized curvature equation gives 𝐸Λ ℎ = 0. The kernel of the map from ( 𝑢, 𝑣 ) to ℎ

Proof. The torsion equation is algebraic in the antisymmetric part of the covariant derivative of 𝑢 and determines

              Matter stress tensor and the Einstein

Its classical component is the stress tensor.

Theorem 180.1 (Conservation from the master equation). The coupled BV action

                                        𝑆tot = 𝑆grav + 𝑆matt + ⟨ℎ, 𝜇matt ⟩

satisﬁes the classical master equation precisely when the matter moment map is equivariant. The component
of that equation linear in the diffeomorphism ghost is

                                                    ∇ 𝜇𝑇𝜇𝜈 = 0.

Proof. The master bracket of the coupling with itself is the defect of equivariance. The bracket with the gravi-
tational action is the infinitesimal diffeomorphism of the moment map. Their cancellation is the Hamiltonian
equivariance identity. Pairing the identity with the diffeomorphism ghost and integrating by parts gives stress-tensor
conservation.




                                                                                                                 455
                                                                                         CHAPTER
                                                                                                                  181

            One-loop gravity and analytic torsion
Expand Chern–Simons theory around an acyclic flat connection A. Gauge fixing gives the Laplacians
of the flat adjoint de Rham complex.

Theorem 181.1 (One-loop determinant). The absolute value of the one-loop partition function is the square
root of the Ray–Singer torsion of the ﬂat adjoint bundle:

                                     | 𝑍 1-loop (A)| = 𝜏RS ( 𝑀 , ad A) 1/2 .

The phase is the eta-invariant contribution ﬁxed by the framing convention.
Proof. Bosonic and ghost Gaussian integrals give alternating determinants of the Laplacians in the flat de Rham
complex. Their product is the analytic torsion. The odd signature operator supplies the determinant phase. This
is the standard stationary-phase calculation for Chern–Simons theory [69, 74].

   The same determinant complex is obtained from the Einstein–Cartan, metric, and Calabi pre-
sentations through their chain equivalences. One-loop equality is therefore a consequence of quasi-
isomorphism together with compatible determinant-line orientations.

            Higher-spin holonomy and boundary
                                       charges
In 𝔰𝔩 𝑁 Chern–Simons gravity, a smooth Euclidean saddle is selected by the conjugacy class of the thermal
holonomy. The invariant polynomials

                                         Tr(Hol 𝑠 ) ,      2 ≤ 𝑠 ≤ 𝑁,

fix the spin- 𝑠 charges. Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction identifies the same charges with zero modes of the
𝑊𝑁 currents.

Theorem 183.1 (Bulk–boundary charge equality). For a principal 𝔰𝔩 𝑁 connection in Drinfeld–Sokolov
gauge, the invariant polynomials of the spatial holonomy equal the classical 𝑊𝑁 zero-mode Hamiltonians.
Their Poisson brackets are the classical 𝑊𝑁 brackets.
Proof. Drinfeld–Sokolov gauge writes the connection as the principal raising operator plus lowest-weight compo-
nents. The coefficients of the characteristic polynomial are the invariant polynomials and are polynomial functions
of those components. Hamiltonian reduction gives the 𝑊𝑁 Poisson structure on the same functions.

                                       Four native anomaly classes
The construction carries four curvature classes.
   (i) A local BV anomaly lies in the degree-one local deformation complex.
  (ii) A projective anomaly is the curvature of the determinant line over parameter space.
 (iii) A framing anomaly is the transgression of a characteristic class to a three-manifold.
 (iv) A curved Koszul term is the arity-zero component of a curved coalgebra.

Theorem 184.1 (Curvature-killing extension). Let 𝜅 be a closed central degree-two class in the deformation
complex of a theory. Adjoining a degree-one generator 𝑢 with 𝑑𝑢 = 𝜅 produces a central extension in which
the lifted curvature vanishes. Tensoring with the inverse invertible ﬁeld theory gives the geometric realization
of the same cancellation.
Proof. The extended differential squares to zero because 𝑑𝜅 = 0 and 𝜅 is central. The new Maurer–Cartan
equation contains the compensating field 𝑢. An invertible theory with curvature −𝜅 adds the opposite class under
tensor product.

                                    Hall and Calabi–Yau sources
Let C be a Calabi–Yau category with a Hall moduli problem. An oriented extension correspondence
gives a Hall algebra 𝐻 + . A factorization pushforward to the boundary curve gives a chiral-convolution
algebra. Relative induction supplies the remaining colours.
Theorem 185.1 (Source-to-BV functor). A morphism from the Hall boundary datum to a complete mixed
boundary datum induces morphisms
             𝐻 + −→ 𝐴ch ,          𝑍 Hall −→ 𝑍 mix (𝔸) ,         BVEnvHall −→ BVEnvfull
                                                                                   3 (𝔸) .

Compatibility with coproduct, pairing, and braiding propagates to the quantum line category. Compatibility
with cyclic traces propagates to the quantum and modular amplitudes.
Proof. The Hall map preserves associative multiplication. Relative operadic induction extends it to the mixed
colours and interface. Natural transformation of module actions gives the centre map. Functoriality of convolution
deformation algebras and shifted cotangent gives the BV map. The remaining structures are natural under monoidal
and cyclic functors.

    For 𝑋 = 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 , Volume IV constructs the geometric BPS Borel 𝔟 𝑋 , the Igusa Borcherds Borel
𝔟Δ , and their completed Lie amalgam. Applying the enveloping functor gives the cocommutative Hopf
algebra
                                   𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝑈b (𝔟 𝑋 )b         b (𝔟Δ ) .
                                                    ∗𝑈b (𝔥Π ) 𝑈
The two Hopf retractions preserve the geometric and automorphic sectors separately. A 𝑞 -deformed
mixed Hopf algebra is supplied by the quantum amalgam theorem when a geometric Hall coproduct
and pairing are present.
Theorem 185.2 (Amalgamated source envelope). Relative mixed induction sends 𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ to a complete
mixed boundary datum 𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ . Its universal BV envelope has two canonical retractions
       BVEnvfull                full
            3 (𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ ) −→ BVEnv3 (𝔸 𝑋 ) ,                  BVEnvfull                full
                                                                 3 (𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ ) −→ BVEnv3 (𝔸Δ ) .

A primitive comparison quotient induces the diagonal BV theory in which geometric BPS primitives and
Igusa roots act as one ﬁeld. The kernel of the quotient is the complete mixed interaction ideal.
Proof. Relative induction preserves coproducts and the common Cartan action, so the amalgamated Hopf algebra
produces the amalgamated mixed datum. The Hopf retractions induce maps of mixed data. Functoriality of
the centre, convolution deformation algebra, and shifted cotangent transgression gives the two BV retractions.
A primitive comparison quotient is a quotient of mixed data, and functoriality carries its defining ideal to the
corresponding ideal in the deformation and BV complexes.

                                The Igusa protected insertion
Let 𝔎Δ be the Borcherds–Ginzburg protected category of Volume III. Its ordinary Hall boundary is
the quiver Hall category, its parity-refined line symmetry is the quantized Borcherds superalgebra, its
local–wrapped current object lies on the elliptic curve, and its first-order determinant is the oriented
virtual-root Pfaffian.

Theorem 186.1 (Igusa insertion into the universal envelope). The protected category 𝔎Δ deﬁnes a complete
mixed boundary datum on the elliptic curve. Put DenΔ ( 𝑍 ) = 𝐷5 (2 𝑍 ) and 𝚥 ( 𝑍 ) = 𝑍 /2. Its universal BV

The doubled half-coordinate trace is −( 𝚥∗ DenΔ ) −2 = − 𝜒10−1 in the reduced enumerative normalization.
Proof. The colored Ran current algebra supplies the chiral and transverse colours. The Hall category supplies
ordered particle fusion and the interface to the three-Calabi–Yau completion. The quantum double supplies
exchange. The root Dirac family supplies the determinant. The identities follow from the Borcherds denominator
and the primitive reduced 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 theorem.

The complete theory uses the carrier set
                               ℭ = {diag, ch, ⊥, int, ex, cyc, mod, BV, Ein} .
For a subset 𝑆 ⊂ ℭ, let Str 𝑆 (𝔸) be the homotopy limit of all mapping spaces attached to incidence cells
supported in 𝑆 .
Theorem 187.1 (Carrier descent). When 𝑆 and 𝑇 form a relation-excisive pair, the square

                                       Str 𝑆 ∪𝑇 (𝔸)                Str 𝑆 (𝔸)


                                         Str𝑇 (𝔸)                 Str 𝑆 ∩𝑇 (𝔸)
is a homotopy pullback. Thus a global structure is assembled from its carrier restrictions together with their
common interface.
Proof. Relation-excision identifies the incidence category of 𝑆 ∪ 𝑇 with the pushout of the incidence categories
for 𝑆 and 𝑇 over their intersection. Homotopy limits send this pushout to the displayed pullback.

Proof. An inclusion 𝑆 ⊆ 𝑇 induces the full inclusion I𝑆 ⊆ I𝑇 and therefore the restriction map on homotopy

                                                  Sixteen structural laws
The theory is governed by the following laws.
  (i) A geometric carrier determines the type of its operation.
  (ii) The diagonal stores the complete principal-part filtration.
 (iii) The interval stores ordered fusion.
 (iv) The plane stores exchange.
  (v) The circle stores trace.
 (vi) The stable curve stores modular sewing.
 (vii) The transverse, associative-chiral, and chiral-Lie bars are distinct.
(viii) Chiral PBW compares the last two on enveloping objects.
 (ix) The interface bar records the mixed distributive law.
  (x) The derived centre is the universal interior action.
 (xi) The deformation algebra is the tangent algebra of the centre-extended datum.
 (xii) Maurer–Cartan curvature is the field equation.
(xiii) The 𝐿∞ identity is the Bianchi identity.
(xiv) Shifted cotangent is the universal classical BV envelope.
 (xv) Hodge-completed derived de Rham theory is the intrinsic algebraic quantum complex of the
      oriented formal moduli problem.
(xvi) Einstein completion is projective matching followed by gravitational cotangent transgression.

                                                                                   CHAPTER
                                                                                                          189

                                                                       Final synthesis
The construction proceeds by a single chain of functors:

                                             Def
                                               full        𝑇 ∗ [ −1]
                                                    −−−−−−→ BVEnvfull
                           𝑍 mix
                       𝔸 −−−−→ 𝑍 mix (𝔸) −−−→ 𝔩𝔸                 3 (𝔸) ,

                                         projective matching
                         BVEnvfull
                              3 (𝔸) −
                                    −−−−−−−−−−−−−→ EinBV3 ( 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴 𝑅 ) .
Hall and Calabi–Yau sources enter on the left. Quantum groups enter through planar exchange and the
line category. Automorphic products enter as characters of root-graded protected objects. Each arrow
preserves the structure carried by its source and adds the structure specified by its geometric target.

                                                             Low-arity mixed signs
Let 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 be a suspended interface word. The two length-lowering contractions are

                 𝑏 𝐿 [ 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 ] = (−1) | 𝑥 | ( 𝑥𝑘 )| 𝑦,    𝑏 𝑅 [ 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 ] = (−1) | 𝑥 |+| 𝑘 | 𝑥 |( 𝑘𝑦 ) .

                                     det( 𝐸ch ⊕ 𝐸⊥ ) = det( 𝐸ch ) ⊗ det( 𝐸⊥ ) ,

so reversing their order contributes the Koszul sign and yields anticommutation.

        Five carriers, three noncommutativities,
                               one type discipline
193.1    The local experiment
Let 𝑋 be a smooth complex algebraic curve. Place observables in a product chart 𝑈 × 𝐼 ⊂ 𝑋 an × ℝ,
with 𝑈 a small disc and 𝐼 an oriented interval. Two independent short-distance motions are visible
before any algebra is chosen.
   In the first motion, the points in 𝑈 collide while their interval positions remain fixed. The result is
not merely a residue. It is a distribution supported on a partial diagonal, with all normal jets and their
coordinate-change law. In the second motion, two subintervals become adjacent while the points of 𝑈
remain separated. The result is an ordered associative composition. Thickening 𝐼 to a plane permits one
interval to pass around another and creates braid transport. Closing the interval creates a circle trace.
Allowing the complex curve to degenerate creates clutching and sewing.
   Thus the five carriers are

                    diagonal,        interval,      plane,       circle,      stable curve.

Their native algebraic structures are, respectively, a chiral distribution, an E1 -product, braid transport,
Hochschild/cyclic trace, and a modular action. They are related by geometry, but none is a synonym for
another.

Theorem 193.1 (Stokes-to-master principle). Let 𝐶 be an oriented manifold with corners, let ( 𝐸, 𝑑 𝐸 ) be
a cochain complex, and assign to every oriented codimension-one face 𝐹 an odd operator 𝜌 𝐹 on 𝐸 . Assume
the assignment restricts functorially to corners and

                                             𝑑 𝐸 𝜌 𝐹 + 𝜌 𝐹 𝑑 𝐸 = 0.

For 𝐷 = 𝑑 𝐸 + 𝐹 𝜌 𝐹 , the component of 𝐷 2 supported on a codimension-two stratum 𝑆 is the oriented sum
                P
of the composites 𝜌 𝐹2 𝜌 𝐹1 over ﬂags 𝑆 ⊂ 𝐹2 ⊂ 𝐶 . Hence 𝐷 2 = 0 exactly when every corner identity holds.
Proof. Expand 𝐷 2 . The internal square vanishes and the mixed terms cancel by the chain-map hypothesis. The
remaining terms are ordered pairs of incident faces. Grouping by the common codimension-two stratum produces
the cellular coefficient of 𝜕 2 𝐶 , and the two induced corner orientations are opposite. This is the entire sign
mechanism. The algebraic content enters only through the operators decorating the faces.



                                                                                                            487
193. Five carriers, three noncommutativities, one type discipline                                               488


   Associativity, chiral Jacobi or chiral associativity, the Yang–Baxter equation, the BV master equation,
and modular sewing all have this form, but they live on different compactified parameter spaces. A
universal boundary argument explains the shape of the equations; it does not identify their coefficient
maps.

193.2    The Ran coefficient and its two products
        surj
Let Fin≠∅ denote nonempty finite sets and surjections. A surjection 𝛼 : 𝐼 ↠ 𝐽 determines a partial
diagonal
                      Δ 𝛼 : 𝑋 𝐽 −→ 𝑋 𝐼 ,        ( 𝑥𝑗 )𝑗 ∈ 𝐽 ↦−→ ( 𝑥 𝛼 ( 𝑖 ) ) 𝑖 ∈ 𝐼 .
A right 𝐷 -module on Ran 𝑋 is represented by a coherent family 𝐹𝐼 ∈ DMod( 𝑋 𝐼 ) with extraordinary
pullback identifications along these diagonals. The unital completion adjoins the empty set.
    For a partition 𝜋 = { 𝐵 1 , . . . , 𝐵 𝑟 } of 𝐼 , let 𝑈𝜋 ⊂ 𝑋 𝐼 be the locus where points in distinct blocks are
disjoint, and write 𝑗𝜋 : 𝑈𝜋 ↩→ 𝑋 𝐼 .

Definition 193.2 (Factorisable coefficient). A factorisable right 𝐷 -module is a Ran family 𝐹 equipped
with coherent equivalences                                    
                                             𝑗𝜋! 𝐹𝐼 ≃ 𝑗𝜋! ⊠ 𝐵 ∈ 𝜋 𝐹𝐵
for every partition, compatible with refinement, permutation, and diagonal transition maps. The chosen
stable presentable enhancement is denoted FactD( 𝑋 ).

   There are two monoidal products.
   The pointwise coefficient product is

                                            ( 𝐹 ⊗! 𝐺 ) 𝐼 = 𝐹𝐼 ⊗ ! 𝐺 𝐼 .

It does not add support cardinalities. It is the product in which endomorphisms of a fixed boundary
condition compose.
    Chiral convolution is the Day convolution for disjoint union:

                                                    ( 𝑗𝐼1 ,𝐼2 )∗ 𝑗𝐼!1 ,𝐼2 ( 𝐹𝐼1 ⊠ 𝐺 𝐼2 ) .
                                               M
                             ( 𝐹 ★ch 𝐺 ) 𝐼 =
                                                 𝐼 =𝐼1 ⊔ 𝐼2

It adds support cardinalities and is the ambient product for the Ran-space chiral Chevalley theory.

Proposition 193.3 (Cardinality pro-nilpotence). Let R ≥ 𝑟 be Ran objects whose components vanish below
cardinality 𝑟 . Then
                                       R ≥ 𝑟 ★ch R ≥ 𝑠 ⊆ R ≥ 𝑟 + 𝑠 .
Consequently, reduced chiral convolution is nilpotent modulo cardinality > 𝑁 , and its inverse limit is
pro-nilpotent.
Proof. Every summand is indexed by a disjoint union of the supporting finite sets, so cardinalities add. An ( 𝑁 + 1)-
fold product of reduced objects has support cardinality at least 𝑁 + 1 and vanishes in the 𝑁 -th quotient.

    The same statement is false for ⊗! , whose cardinality degree is zero. This elementary distinction is the
first reason that the two bar constructions must not be conflated.
