                                                                                                            213
Perturbative BV realization of the ordered
                              chiral object
213.1   Classical data
A perturbative classical field theory on a manifold 𝑀 is described locally by an elliptic L∞ -algebra of
fields E [−1], an invariant pairing of degree −1, and an action functional 𝑆 solving the classical master
equation
                                                  1
                                           𝑑𝑆 + { 𝑆, 𝑆 } = 0.
                                                  2
Classical observables on an open set are Chevalley cochains of compactly supported fields, with differen-
tial induced by the L∞ -structure. Disjoint support gives factorisation multiplication.
    On 𝑋 an × ℝ, assume the kinetic complex is Dolbeault in 𝑋 and de Rham or locally constant in the
real direction. A boundary condition on 𝑋 × ℝ ≥0 restricts fields to a factorisation algebra on 𝑋 × ℝ.
The ordered interval direction then supplies a transverse E1 -product.

213.2   Renormalised quantum observables
A gauge fixing produces a propagator 𝑃𝜖𝐿 between ultraviolet and infrared scales. Effective interactions
𝐼 [ 𝐿 ] solve the homotopy renormalisation-group equation and the quantum master equation

                                         ( 𝑄 + ℏΔ 𝐿 ) 𝑒 𝐼 [ 𝐿 ]/ℏ = 0.

The quantum observable differential is

                                     𝑄 𝐿 = 𝑄 + { 𝐼 [ 𝐿 ] , −} 𝐿 + ℏΔ 𝐿 .

The QME is exactly 𝑄 2𝐿 = 0.
   Feynman weights are differential forms on configuration spaces of vertices. Renormalisation extends
them to compactified collision spaces after local counterterms are added. Restriction to a boundary face
factorises through contraction of the corresponding subgraph.

Theorem 213.1 (Feynman-weight morphism). Suppose:
   (i) renormalised graph weights extend to the chosen two-direction compactiﬁcations;
  (ii) restriction to every face agrees with graph contraction and the relevant local counterterm;



                                                                                                     543
213. Perturbative BV realization of the ordered chiral object                                                544


 (iii) the renormalised QME holds;
  (iv) boundary observables are holomorphically algebraizable on 𝑋 and locally constant in the ordered
       direction.
Then the graph weights deﬁne a morphism from the geometric two-coloured forest resolution to the endo-
morphism operad of boundary observables. Hence the boundary observables form a homotopy-coherent
transversely ordered chiral algebra. If ordered chiral collision vertices are also present, they form the corre-
sponding doubly associative object.
Proof. Face factorisation is operadic composition. Stokes’ theorem for the extended weights identifies the de
Rham boundary with the sum of graph contractions. The QME cancels the full codimension-one boundary,
including BV and counterterm faces, so the assignment is a chain map. Algebraisation and local constancy identify
the resulting operations with the coefficient and transverse structures.

No-go. A classical factorisation algebra can fail to admit a quantum lift because the one-loop obstruction
class is nonzero. Gauge ﬁxing can fail to preserve the chosen boundary condition, and the analytic observables
can fail to algebraize as regular holonomic 𝐷 -modules. The quantum ordered chiral object exists only after
these obstructions vanish or are cancelled by an invertible anomaly theory.


213.3    Free holomorphic–topological BF theory
Let 𝑉 be finite dimensional and take fields

                 𝐴 ∈ Ω0,∗ ( 𝑋 ) ⊗
                                bΩ∗ (ℝ) ⊗ 𝑉 [1] ,            𝐵 ∈ Ω1,∗ ( 𝑋 ) ⊗
                                                                            bΩ∗ (ℝ) ⊗ 𝑉 ∗ [−1]

with action                                     ∫
                                        𝑆BF =           ⟨ 𝐵, ( 𝜕¯ + 𝑑𝑡 ) 𝐴⟩ .
                                                 𝑋 ×ℝ
The theory is Gaussian. The de Rham contraction on an interval proves local constancy in 𝑡 ; the Dol-
beault complex gives the holomorphic coefficient. Complementary linear boundary conditions select a
polarization. Quantization of paired boundary fields produces chiral Weyl or Clifford systems according
to parity. The central constant in the Weyl relation is handled by the curved duality of chapter 205, not
by a nonexistent scalar augmentation.

214.1    The bar–BRST bicomplex
Let 𝑄 : 𝐴 → 𝐴 [1] be a square-zero derivation of the transverse multiplication, augmentation, and
every factorisation map. On a bar word define
                                                     𝑟

                                                                  P
                       𝑄 𝐵 [ 𝑎1 | · · · | 𝑎 𝑟 ] =
                                                    𝑖 =1

Proposition 214.1 (BRST compatibility).

                                         𝑄 𝐵2 = 0,                𝑄 𝐵 𝑑 𝐵 + 𝑑 𝐵 𝑄 𝐵 = 0.

Thus (Bar( 𝐴) , 𝑑𝐵 , 𝑄 𝐵 ) is a bicomplex in factorisation coeﬃcients.
Proof. The first identity is the tensor differential calculation. For the second, compare applying 𝑄 before and after
an interior face. The two terms agree by the derivation rule and acquire opposite totalisation signs. Outer faces
agree because the augmentation is 𝑄 -closed.

Theorem 214.2 (Bar–BRST comparison). Assume tensor products preserve 𝑄 -quasi-isomorphisms and
the combined ﬁltration is complete and bounded below in every total weight. Then

                            𝐸1  Bar 𝐻𝑄 ( 𝐴) =⇒ 𝐻 ∗ Tot(Bar( 𝐴) , 𝑑𝐵 + 𝑄 𝐵 ) .
                                            


If 𝐻𝑄 ( 𝐴) is concentrated in degree zero, the edge map
                                                              ∼
                                    Bar( 𝐻𝑄0 ( 𝐴)) −
                                                   → Tot(Bar( 𝐴) , 𝑑𝐵 + 𝑄 𝐵 )

is a quasi-isomorphism.
Proof. Filter first by BRST degree and then by bar length. The vertical complex is a tensor product of copies
of ( 𝐴, 𝑄 ). The Kunneth hypothesis identifies its cohomology with tensor powers of 𝐻𝑄 ( 𝐴), and the induced
horizontal differential is the bar differential of the cohomology algebra. Completeness gives convergence. Concen-
tration leaves one row.

214.2    Principal Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction
For an affine vertex algebra 𝑉𝑘 (𝔤), choose a good grading for a nilpotent element 𝑓 , introduce charged
and neutral ghosts, and form the BRST current from the moment map, cubic ghost term, character,
and neutral correction. Its residue is a derivation 𝑄 with 𝑄 2 = 0. The reduced algebra is

                                    W𝑘 (𝔤, 𝑓 ) = 𝐻𝑄0 (𝑉𝑘 (𝔤) ⊗ Ghosts) .

For principal 𝑓 at generic noncritical level, the standard concentration theorem places cohomology
in degree zero. Then theorem 214.2 identifies the ordered bar of the principal 𝑊 -algebra with BRST
cohomology of the affine-ghost bar.

214.3    The 𝔰𝔩 2 calculation
For affine currents 𝑒, ℎ, 𝑓 at level 𝑘 , introduce fermionic ghosts 𝑏, 𝑐 and

                         𝑗BRST ( 𝑧 ) = ( 𝑒 ( 𝑧 ) − 1) 𝑐 ( 𝑧 ) ,       𝑄 = Res 𝑗BRST ( 𝑧 ) .

The 𝑒 -𝑒 and 𝑐 -𝑐 OPEs are regular, hence 𝑄 2 = 0. Improving the Sugawara stress tensor and adding the
ghost stress tensor gives a 𝑄 -closed class with central charge

                                                                   6      6( 𝑘 + 1) 2
                            𝑐DS ( 𝑘 ) = 13 − 6( 𝑘 + 2) −              =1−             .
                                                                  𝑘+2       𝑘+2
A filtration by 𝑓 - and 𝑏 -modes pairs the constrained currents into contractible quartets. The surviving
Heisenberg field has the Feigin–Fuchs background charge, and its invariant Miura subalgebra is Virasoro.
This realizes principal 𝔰𝔩 2 reduction in degree zero for generic 𝑘 ≠ −2.
Warning 214.3. At special levels, higher BRST cohomology and nonsemisimple phenomena can occur.
The concentration hypothesis must be checked in the representation category being used.

         From order to exchange: KZ, Tannaka,

215.1    Infinitesimal braid relations
Let 𝔤 be a quadratic Lie algebra and Ω ∈ 𝔤 ⊗ 𝔤 the inverse invariant form. On 𝑉1 ⊗ · · · ⊗ 𝑉𝑛 , write Ω𝑖𝑗
for its action in factors 𝑖, 𝑗 . Invariance gives

                     [ Ω𝑖𝑗 , Ω𝑘𝑙 ] = 0   for disjoint pairs,          [ Ω𝑖𝑗 , Ω𝑖𝑘 + Ω𝑗 𝑘 ] = 0.

These are the infinitesimal braid relations.
  The KZ connection on Conf 𝑛 (ℂ) is

                                                           Ω𝑖𝑗 𝑑 log( 𝑧 𝑖 − 𝑧𝑗 ) .
                                                    ∑︁
                                    ∇KZ = 𝑑 − 𝜅
                                                    𝑖 <𝑗

Proposition 215.1 (KZ flatness). The KZ connection is ﬂat.
Proof. The logarithmic one-forms are closed, so curvature is the wedge-square of the connection form. Terms
on four distinct labels vanish by disjoint commutativity. On three labels, the coefficient is an infinitesimal braid
commutator multiplied by Arnold’s relation

                                     𝜔12 ∧ 𝜔23 + 𝜔23 ∧ 𝜔31 + 𝜔31 ∧ 𝜔12 = 0.

Both factors vanish in the required combination.

    Monodromy gives a braid-group representation. The Drinfeld–Kohno theorem identifies the formal
monodromy category with the braided representation category of the corresponding quantum group
after an associator and parameter identification are chosen. The associator is part of the comparison; it is
not produced by the ordered line.

215.2    Meromorphic Tannaka reconstruction
Let C be a rigid meromorphic tensor category of line operators and let 𝜔 : C → Vect𝕜 ( ( 𝑢 ) ) be a faithful
exact strong monoidal fibre functor. The coend
                                               ∫ 𝑉 ∈C
                                         𝐻=                𝜔 (𝑉 ) ∗ ⊗ 𝜔 (𝑉 )


                                                                                                                547
215. From order to exchange: KZ, Tannaka, and four-dimensional Chern–Simons                                     548


reconstructs a bialgebra. Rigidity gives an antipode. Braided exchange gives a coquasitriangular structure.
A topological completion may be required when spectral expansions are infinite.
No-go. Tannaka reconstruction outputs an ordinary or topological Hopf algebra only after a suitable ﬁbre
functor is speciﬁed. Without it, the intrinsic output is the tensor category itself, or a Hopf object in a different
base category. ‘‘The quantum group’’ is not a scalar extracted from braid matrices.


215.3    Four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory
Let Σ be an oriented real surface, 𝐶 a complex curve with meromorphic one-form 𝜔, and consider the
partial connection of four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory on Σ × 𝐶 :

                                     1                         2
                                        ∫                                 
                       𝑆4𝑑 ( 𝐴) =             𝜔 ∧ Tr 𝐴 ∧ 𝑑𝐴 + 𝐴 ∧ 𝐴 ∧ 𝐴 .
                                   2 𝜋 ℏ Σ ×𝐶                  3

The theory is topological on Σ and holomorphic on 𝐶 . A Wilson line in Σ at spectral point 𝑧 ∈ 𝐶 is
a line object. Parallel fusion is ordered; exchange in Σ produces an 𝑅-matrix. In the rational case, the
tree-level exchange is
                                                             Ω
                                    𝑅 ( 𝑧1 − 𝑧2 ) = 1 + ℏ         + 𝑂 (ℏ2 ) .
                                                          𝑧1 − 𝑧2
Gauge invariance at the first nontrivial order imposes the classical Yang–Baxter equation. Quantisation
produces the rational Yangian-type exchange algebra under the boundary, anomaly, and representation
hypotheses of the model.
   The geometry explains why ZF/RTT relations and Yang–Baxter occur. The complete identification
with a specific Yangian remains a theorem about the line category and its generators, not a consequence
of dimensional analysis.

216.1    Closing the transverse interval
Let 𝐴 be a proper cyclic A∞ -algebra object in the coefficient category. A degree- 𝑑 Calabi–Yau structure
is a negative-cyclic class inducing an equivalence
                                                        ∼
                                                     → 𝐴∨ [ 𝑑 ] .
                                                    𝐴−

On a finite strict model it is represented by a cyclic trace with perfect pairing ⟨ 𝑎, 𝑏 ⟩ = Tr 𝐴 ( 𝑎𝑏 ). The
circle value is                            ∫
                                                𝐴 ≃ HH∗ ( 𝐴) .
                                                  𝑆1
A ribbon graph is evaluated by cyclic higher multiplications at vertices and the inverse pairing at edges.

Theorem 216.1 (Ribbon-graph action). A ﬁnite perfect cyclic A∞ -algebra deﬁnes a dg PROP morphism
from the positive-boundary ribbon-graph complex to endomorphisms of its Hochschild chains. Edge con-
traction is sent to composition of cyclic operations, and gluing boundary circles is sent to insertion of the
coevaluation pairing.
Proof. The graph evaluation is the tensor contraction determined by cyclic order at vertices. The ribbon differential
sums internal-edge contractions. The cyclic A∞ -relations are exactly the assertion that this sum intertwines the
graph and Hochschild differentials. Disjoint union and gluing agree with tensor product and coevaluation.

   This produces a transverse ribbon genus 𝑔⊥ .

216.2     Chiral sewing over stable curves
A modular chiral coefficient assigns state spaces or complexes to pointed curves and contraction maps to
separating and nonseparating clutching morphisms

                                      M 𝑔1 ,𝑛1 +1 × M 𝑔2 ,𝑛2 +1 𝑜M 𝑔1 + 𝑔2 ,𝑛1 +𝑛2 ,

                                               M 𝑔 −1,𝑛+2 → M 𝑔,𝑛 .
A perfect chiral pairing contracts the two flags of a node. The resulting genus 𝑔 𝑋 is the genus of the
algebraic curve and has no reason to equal 𝑔⊥ .


                                                                                                                 549
216. Cyclic traces, modular sewing, and the two-edge master equation                                           550


216.3    Bicoloured stable graphs
Give every internal edge a colour 𝑋 or ⊥. An 𝑋 -edge is a chiral clutching node; a ⊥-edge is a ribbon/trace
contraction. Orient a graph by
                                           det 𝐸𝑋 ⊗ det 𝐸⊥ .
Exchanging the two edge colours changes sign. The graph brackets and loop operators are

                                    [−, −] 𝑋 ,   [−, −] ⊥ ,      Δ𝑋 ,     Δ⊥ .

Recognition Theorem 216.2 (Two-edge sewing theorem). Suppose a state object carries:
   (i) cyclic transverse gluing and a perfect transverse pairing;
  (ii) chiral clutching and a perfect chiral sewing pairing;
 (iii) common genus-zero corollas;
 (iv) the one-colour associativity/modular identities;
  (v) a mixed interchange square for one ribbon and one chiral edge, with the product determinant sign.
Then there is a unique action of the bicoloured stable-graph transform extending the two one-colour actions.
Its master element satisﬁes
                           1          1
                       𝑑Θ + [Θ, Θ] 𝑋 + [Θ, Θ] ⊥ + ℏ 𝑋 Δ 𝑋 Θ + ℏ⊥ Δ⊥ Θ = 0.
                           2          2
In particular,
                                            Δ 𝑋 Δ⊥ + Δ⊥ Δ 𝑋 = 0.
Proof. Every bicoloured graph is built by gluing common corollas along coloured edges. The one-colour axioms
identify all decompositions involving only one colour. The mixed square identifies adjacent contractions of differ-
ent colours. These moves connect any two edge-orderings, giving existence and uniqueness. The codimension-two
boundary of the bicoloured graph complex is the sum of the two brackets, two loop contractions, and the internal
differential. Its square is zero by the determinant orientation, yielding the master equation.

No-go. The ribbon genus counts handles in the surface thickening of a cyclic associative graph. The chiral
genus counts handles of the algebraic curve in Deligne–Mumford moduli. They are gradings from different
edge colours. A concrete duality may relate selected amplitudes, but there is no natural transformation
identifying 𝑔 𝑋 with 𝑔⊥ for all ordered chiral algebras.

    Prime forms, plumbing, and the analytic

217.1   The normalized bidifferential
Let Σ 𝑔 be a compact Riemann surface with symplectic homology basis and period matrix Ω. The prime
form 𝐸 ( 𝑝, 𝑞 ) is a (−1/2, −1/2)-form with a simple zero on the diagonal. The normalized fundamental
bidifferential has local behaviour

                            𝐵 ( 𝑝, 𝑞 ) =                         + 𝑂 (1) 𝑑𝑧 ( 𝑝) 𝑑𝑧 ( 𝑞 )

and vanishing 𝐴-periods. It may be expressed through logarithmic derivatives of the prime form with

   For a rank-one Heisenberg current,

                                     ⟨ 𝐽 ( 𝑝) 𝐽 ( 𝑞 )⟩Σ 𝑔 = 𝑘𝐵 ( 𝑝, 𝑞 ) .

Higher current correlators are sums over pairings. This is an exact Gaussian realization of the chiral
coefficient at every genus once the determinant line and zero modes are included.

For local coordinates 𝑧, 𝑤 at two marked points, plumbing identifies

                                          𝑧𝑤 = 𝑞,         | 𝑞 | ≪ 1.

A separating node joins two components; a nonseparating node joins two points on one component.
Formal sewing inserts a complete set of states:
                                       ∑︁ 𝐿 ( 𝛼 ) − 𝑐 /24

The expression is formal in 𝑞 . Analytic sewing additionally requires convergence, control of the comple-
tion, and compatibility of the chosen local coordinates.
No-go. An identity in 𝑉 [[ 𝑞 ]] can hold while the corresponding series has zero radius of convergence in
a chosen topology. Trace-class estimates, energy bounds, or a rational/CFT ﬁniteness theorem are separate
analytic inputs. Formal modular-operad structure and analytic conformal blocks must be stated separately.

218.1    The transverse Frobenius algebra

This is an exact closed-surface amplitude in the associated two-dimensional topological field theory

218.2    A rank-one Deligne–Mumford theory
Let 𝐶𝜆 = 𝕜1 be the one-dimensional commutative Frobenius algebra with

                                       1 · 1 = 1,         𝜂 (1, 1) = 𝜆 ∈ 𝕜 × .

The inverse metric is 𝜆 −1 , and the handle element is 𝜆 −1 1. The associated rank-one cohomological field
theory assigns the constant class

                                        Ω 𝑔,𝑟 = 𝜆1− 𝑔 ∈ 𝐻 0 (M 𝑔,𝑟 ; 𝕜)

to every stable pointed curve.

Proposition 218.1 (Clutching). The classes Ω 𝑔,𝑟 satisfy both separating and nonseparating CohFT gluing
equations.
Proof. A separating gluing contracts two classes with the inverse metric and gives

                                          𝜆1− 𝑔1 𝜆1− 𝑔2 𝜆 −1 = 𝜆1− ( 𝑔1 + 𝑔2 ) .

A nonseparating gluing gives
                                              𝜆1− ( 𝑔 −1) 𝜆 −1 = 𝜆1− 𝑔 .
The unit axiom is immediate.




                                                                                                       553
218. A concrete two-genus model                                                                           554


218.3    Strict mixed interchange
Form the tensor-product state object
                                               H = 𝐵 𝑛 ⊗ 𝐶𝜆 .
Ribbon contractions act on the first factor; Deligne–Mumford clutching acts on the second. Common
corollas are tensor products of the two multiplications. Operators on different tensor factors commute
before the determinant-line sign is inserted.

Theorem 218.2 (Realized two-edge recognition). The state object H satisﬁes all ﬁve hypotheses of recogni-
tion Theorem 216.2. Its bivariate closed amplitude is

                                      𝑍 𝑔𝑋 , 𝑔⊥ ( 𝑛, 𝜆) = 𝜆1− 𝑔𝑋 𝑅 𝑔⊥ ( 𝑛) .

Thus

          𝑍 𝑔𝑋 ,0 = 𝑛𝜆1− 𝑔𝑋 ,      𝑍 𝑔𝑋 ,1 = (2 + 𝑛2 ) 𝜆1− 𝑔𝑋 ,        𝑍 𝑔𝑋 ,ℎ = 𝑛ℎ+1 𝜆1− 𝑔𝑋 ( ℎ ≥ 2) .

The two genus variables are independent and the mixed interchange square is strict.
Proof. Cyclicity and perfection for 𝐵 𝑛 give the transverse sewing. proposition 218.1 gives the chiral modular
sewing. Tensor-product corollas satisfy each one-colour associativity law. A contraction on 𝐵 𝑛 commutes with
a contraction on 𝐶𝜆 , and exchanging their odd edge orientations supplies the required sign. The recognition
theorem applies. The amplitude factorises into the two exact one-colour amplitudes.

    This is the missing realized invocation of the recognition theorem. It is deliberately elementary
enough that every hypothesis and scalar can be checked. It proves the formal two-genus architecture
unconditionally. Tensoring the rank-one CohFT with a rational vertex-algebra modular functor gives
richer analytic examples once convergence and anomaly-line hypotheses are supplied.

218.4    What the model proves and what it does not
The example proves existence of a noncommutative transverse sector, a genuine augmentation and
bar, two independent modular contractions, and a mixed graph action. It does not claim that every
holomorphic–topological gauge theory factorizes as a tensor product, or that its two loop parameters are
physically unrelated. Interacting theories can have mixed vertices; then the mixed square is a substantive
calculation rather than strict tensor-factor commutation.




554
                                                                                           CHAPTER
                                                                                                                    219
        Typed anomalies and invertible theories
219.1    Five domains
The word ‘‘anomaly’’ names several obstruction classes.

   Anomaly type                         Native object
   Curved Koszul term                   arity-zero element ℎ in a curved dg algebra, with 𝑑 2 = [ ℎ, −].
   BRST anomaly                         failure of the quantum BRST operator to square to zero, a class
                                        in the BRST deformation complex.
   BV anomaly                           obstruction to the QME, usually a degree-one local functional
                                        modulo exact counterterms.
   Conformal/determinant                curvature or first Chern class of a determinant line over moduli.
   anomaly
   Framing anomaly                      invertible field theory or central extension measuring depen-
                                        dence on a framing/trivialization.

A family index map, transgression, boundary map, or open–closed comparison can carry one class to
another in a concrete theory. The map is the theorem.
Theorem 219.1 (Anomaly cancellation by an inverse theory). Let L be the invertible anomaly theory
of a quantum ﬁeld theory T . If an inverse L −1 and a trivialization of L ⊗ L −1 are chosen compatibly
with gluing, then T ⊗ L −1 has an honest, rather than projective, state assignment on the corresponding
bordism/moduli category.
Proof. The anomalous theory is a relative natural transformation valued in the line assigned by L. Tensoring with
the inverse line makes the value canonically scalar after the chosen trivialization. Compatibility with gluing is
exactly naturality of the trivialization.

No-go. There is no number that simultaneously represents curved Koszul data, BV obstruction, determinant
curvature, BRST failure, and framing dependence. They live in different cohomology theories and can
have different degrees. A scalar appears only after evaluating a speciﬁed class on a cycle, trace, index, or
background.

No-go. The Virasoro central charge is one coeﬃcient of one OPE. Distinct vertex algebras, module cate-
gories, fusion rules, modular data, and transverse products can share the same central charge. Therefore no
classiﬁcation of ordered or associative chiral algebras can be based on 𝑐 alone.

                               Bar⊥ (𝑈 (𝔫+ )) ≃ CE∗ (𝔫+ ) = Sym(𝔫+ [1]) .

                          𝜒Γ Bar⊥ (𝑈 (𝔫+ )) =     (1 − 𝑒 − 𝛼 ) sdim 𝔤 𝛼 .

If 𝔤 is a Borcherds–Kac–Moody superalgebra with Weyl vector 𝜌, multiplying by 𝑒 − 𝜌 gives the product side

Proof. An even root vector becomes odd after suspension and contributes 1 − 𝑒 − 𝛼 to the Euler supercharacter.
An odd root vector becomes even and contributes its inverse. Multiplication over a homogeneous basis gives the
superdimension exponent. Finite decomposition makes each coefficient of the completed product finite.

   The theorem is an object-to-character map: the Lie bracket controls the Chevalley differential, while
the Euler character remembers only signed root multiplicities.

220.3    Finite positive windows
Let ℎ : Γ+ → ℕ>0 be proper and additive. The quotient
                                                   M
                                   𝔫+ ( 𝐻 ) = 𝔫+ /    𝔤𝛼
                                                              ℎ ( 𝛼) >𝐻

is finite-dimensional nilpotent. Its level-zero current vertex algebra has PBW character, and the inverse
limit over 𝐻 is separated and complete. Coefficientwise finiteness follows because a fixed root and
oscillator degree sees only finitely many positive roots and modes.

220.4    Polarized Gram transform
Let Π : Γphys → Λ be quadratic with polarization

                                𝐵 ( 𝛾, 𝛿 ) = Π ( 𝛾 + 𝛿 ) − Π ( 𝛾 ) − Π ( 𝛿 ) .

For a charge-graded algebra 𝐴 =       𝛾 𝐴 𝛾 , define
                                  L


                                ( 𝑎 𝛾 𝑢 𝜆 ) ★𝐵 ( 𝑏 𝛿 𝑢 𝜇 ) = 𝑎 𝛾 𝑏 𝛿 𝑢 𝜆+ 𝜇+ 𝐵 ( 𝛾,𝛿 ) .

Biadditivity gives the cocycle identity and hence associativity. The Gram embedding

                                              𝜄Π ( 𝑎 𝛾 ) = 𝑎 𝛾 𝑢 Π ( 𝛾 )

is an algebra homomorphism for ★𝐵 . This is the exact device that turns quadratic Fourier indices into
an additive grading without pretending that Π itself is additive.
Warning 220.2. A compact Hall realization of the Igusa root algebra is a further theorem. It must
construct the moduli stack, orientation data, extension correspondence, PBW comparison, and map to
the Borcherds algebra. The denominator product alone supplies none of these maps.

                                                                                    CHAPTER
                                                                                                           221
                        Higher-dimensional sources and
                             factorisation pushforward
221.1   Pushforward along an actual map
Let 𝑓 : 𝑀 → 𝑋 be a map and O a factorisation algebra on 𝑀 . Under properness/support and descent
conditions, factorisation pushforward is

                                       ( 𝑓∗Fact O) (𝑈 ) = O ( 𝑓 −1𝑈 ) .

Disjoint opens in 𝑋 have disjoint inverse images, so the factorisation maps descend. Holomorphic or
topological reduction to a curve is therefore a functor only after an actual map and support theory are
specified.

221.2   Calabi–Yau categories and Hall products
A Calabi–Yau category of branes can produce:
  (i) an E1 -algebra of endomorphisms of a chosen brane;
  (ii) extension correspondences on moduli of objects, yielding Hall or cohomological Hall multiplica-
       tion;
 (iii) cyclic pairings and open-string traces;
 (iv) closed-string maps into Hochschild invariants.
Shifted symplectic geometry can orient the correspondences and control quantization. Each arrow needs
its own finiteness and orientation theorem. A slogan such as ‘‘Calabi–Yau source’’ is not yet an ordered
chiral algebra.

221.3   Coulomb branches and shifted Yangians
In suitable supersymmetric gauge theories, convolution on affine Grassmannian-type spaces produces
quantized Coulomb branches, while line defects organize modules over shifted Yangians. To place such
an algebra in the present theory one must exhibit:
  (i) the curve-valued factorisation coefficient;
  (ii) the ordered line multiplication;
 (iii) the augmentation or relative base used by the bar;


                                                                                                    559
221. Higher-dimensional sources and factorisation pushforward                                     560


 (iv) the exchange geometry or 𝑅-matrix;
  (v) the comparison to the RTT/Drinfeld presentation.
Once these data are present, bar, centre, and BRST constructions are functorial. Without them, name
matching is not a proof.

221.4   Source-to-chiral comparison
The correct general pattern is a diagram

                                               pushforward/restriction
            higher-dimensional geometric category                 factorisation category on 𝑋
                Hall or convolution                                                RHom( 𝑏,𝑏 )

                        geometric algebra        comparison
                                                                         AlgE1 (FactD( 𝑋 )) .

Every square is a theorem about correspondences, supports, and descent. The diagram is the disciplined
replacement for a universal ‘‘shadow tower.’’




560
                                                                                  CHAPTER
                                                                                                         222
                                             The no-go constitution
The theory must distinguish a genuine comparison map from a coincidence of shadows. Each prohibition
below names the datum whose absence blocks the proposed identification and the construction that
would make a comparison possible.

222.1   Carrier prohibitions
 N1. Residue is not the transverse bar differential. Residue changes normal/collision degree; the bar
     differential contracts adjacent transverse letters. They can anticommute in a bicomplex.
 N2. A logarithmic form does not extract all principal parts. Higher poles require the complete
     normal-jet filtration or a chosen connection.
 N3. Incidence does not contract screen cohomology. A terminal corolla can make an incidence
     nerve contractible while the geometric screen has nontrivial cohomology.
 N4. Order does not produce exchange. An ordered chamber on a line is contractible and has no
     braid generator.
 N5. A trace is not an augmentation. Cyclic linear functionals do not define multiplicative bar faces.
 N6. Graph topology does not determine coefficients. A stable graph specifies possible contractions;
     a pairing and state assignment specify their values.

222.2   Monoidal and operadic prohibitions
 N7. Hadamard and Cauchy products are not globally strongly duoidal. The exact relation is a split
     inclusion/projection with aligned idempotent.
 N8. Transverse E1 does not imply associative chiral E1 . The two products have different carriers and
     operads.
 N9. Associative order does not imply a quantum vertex S-operator. Unitarity, Yang–Baxter, shift,
     and braided locality are extra equations.
N10. Two pairwise exchange rules do not imply coherent three-field exchange. The critical-pair
     obstruction is Yang–Baxter.
N11. The interval bar, associative chiral bar, and chiral Chevalley complex are not one complex.
     A comparison requires an enveloping or distributive-law map.
N12. A quadratic dual is not automatically the derived Koszul dual. Exactness of the quadratic
     Koszul complex is the missing theorem.


                                                                                                  561
222. The no-go constitution                                                                      562


N13. Bar–cobar is not an equivalence without completion. The output is nilcomple-
     tion/conilcompletion in general.
N14. Verdier, boundary, quadratic, and bar duals are not definitionally identical. Finite self-dual
     examples supply comparison maps.

222.3   Algebra and calculation prohibitions
N15. The Weyl algebra has no scalar augmentation. Use a Rees degeneration, a curved dual, or a
     relative module.
N16. A full matrix algebra has no scalar augmentation. Use Morita/relative bar data or embed it in
     an honestly augmented algebra.
N17. A multi-idempotent quiver is naturally relative over its vertex algebra. Scalar augmentation
     forgets the boundary objects.
N18. A chain-space count is not a homology dimension. The differential and its rank are part of the
     invariant.
N19. A matching integer sequence is not a quasi-isomorphism. Motzkin and Riordan arithmetic
     survives only in its actual planar combinatorial role.
N20. Central charge does not classify a chiral algebra. OPEs, modules, fusion, modular data, and
     transverse multiplication remain independent.
N21. A character does not reconstruct a Hopf algebra. Coproduct, antipode, associator, and ex-
     change are structured maps.
N22. A denominator product does not construct a Hall realization. Moduli, orientations, extension
     correspondences, and PBW comparison are separate.

222.4    Physics and sewing prohibitions
N23. A boundary centre does not determine an arbitrary decoupled bulk. An open–closed equiva-
     lence is a theorem with generation and locality hypotheses.
N24. The two genera are not universally equal. Ribbon and Deligne–Mumford handles are different
     edge colours.
N25. Formal sewing does not imply analytic convergence. Energy bounds or trace-class estimates
     are additional inputs.
N26. Different anomaly classes are not one scalar. Curved, BV, BRST, determinant, and framing
     anomalies live in different complexes.
N27. Classical factorisation does not guarantee quantum factorisation. The QME obstruction and
     boundary anomaly must vanish.
N28. A physical analogy does not supply a functor. Every comparison between gauge theory, Hall
     geometry, vertex algebra, and automorphic form must specify source, target, and map.

222.5   Positive replacements
The constitution is not merely restrictive. Each no-go theorem has a constructive replacement:

      boundary centre equals bulk        bulk-to-centre map plus open–closed theorem
      one genus                          bicoloured stable graphs and mixed recognition

223.2    Finite calculations and their boundaries
For the quantum plane at 𝑞 = 2 and the Jordan plane, normalized bar words are graded by total polyno-
mial weight 𝑤 ≤ 5 and word length. The alternating adjacent-multiplication matrices are rational. Their
nonzero homology in this window is
                                        𝐻1,1  𝕜 2 ,     𝐻2,2  𝕜 ,
with all other groups zero; Appendix 226 gives the bases, matrix ranks, and cycles. For 𝑈 (𝔰𝔩 2 ), the
Chevalley–Eilenberg differential has det 𝑑2 = −4, 𝑑3 = 0, and
                            (dim 𝐻0 , dim 𝐻1 , dim 𝐻2 , dim 𝐻3 ) = (1, 0, 0, 1) .


                                                                                                   565
223. Typed obstruction and finite-calculation ledger                                                    566


These calculations refute universal Motzkin or Riordan formulas for the corresponding ordered bar
homology. A scalar sequence, chain-space count, or finite window does not establish a quasi-isomorphism
outside its stated carrier and range.

223.3    The exact evidentiary standard
A worked example is complete only when it contains:
   (i) the coefficient object and its diagonal extension;
  (ii) the associative product and augmentation or relative base;
 (iii) generators, relations, and a normal-form or PBW theorem;
 (iv) the actual bar or Koszul differential;
  (v) cycles, boundaries, and homology, not only chain dimensions;
 (vi) any exchange, trace, modular, or physical realization as a named extra map;
 (vii) an exact finite-window calculation, with its parameter range, when constants or signs are nontrivial.
The quantum plane, Jordan plane, relation quiver, 𝔰𝔩 2 , ZF system, 𝐴2 Hall algebra, stable trace bracket,
and two-genus Frobenius/CohFT model meet this standard in the present volume.

                                                                                        CHAPTER
                                                                                                              224
                        The definitive structure theorem
224.1    The primitive datum
Let 𝑋 be a smooth complex curve. The complete local object is the tuple
                             𝔸 = 𝐹 , 𝑚⊥ , 𝑚 𝑋 , 𝜆, 𝜀 ; completions ,
                                                                   

where:
  (i) 𝐹 is a factorisable 𝐷 -module carrying every diagonal singularity and regular product;
  (ii) 𝑚⊥ is an augmented transverse E1 -multiplication for ⊗! ;
 (iii) 𝑚 𝑋 is an augmented associative chiral E1 -multiplication for ★ch or Assch
                                                                               𝑋;
 (iv) 𝜆 is the aligned mixed distributive law, including all higher homotopies;
  (v) 𝜀 is a scalar or relative augmentation appropriate to the algebra;
 (vi) the nilpotent, weight, pole, coradical, and analytic completions are specified separately.
Any subset of this tuple defines the corresponding partial theory. No missing component is inferred
from the others.
Theorem 224.1 (Definitive structure theorem). For a complete datum 𝔸 as above, the following construc-
tions are functorial and type preserving.
  (i) Bar⊥ (𝔸) is interval factorisation homology and a conilpotent transverse coalgebra retaining the
      associative chiral coeﬃcient.
  (ii) Barch
          Ass (𝔸) is the operadic associative chiral bar retaining the transverse algebra.
 (iii) The mixed distributive law gives a total two-direction bar with anticommuting edge differentials
       and an aligned Fubini comparison.
 (iv) On the nilcomplete/conilcomplete locus, the corresponding bar and cobar functors are inverse.
  (v) The augmentation boundary, relative module categories, two-sided twisting transform, and derived
      centre form the Koszul–Morita atlas.
 (vi) An E2 -lift or ﬂat meromorphic transport adds exchange; a ﬁbre functor reconstructs the corresponding
      quantum symmetry.
 (vii) A compatible BRST operator forms a bicomplex and descends all commuting structures to cohomology.
(viii) A cyclic transverse pairing and a modular chiral pairing satisfying the mixed square produce the
       two-edge master equation with independent genus variables.


                                                                                                       567
224. The definitive structure theorem                                                                             568


 (ix) A perturbative BV realization gives the preceding object when renormalised graph weights extend,
      the QME holds, and the observables algebraize.
  (x) Root-graded Euler character, trace, period, index, and monodromy maps produce scalar shadows
      only after the structured object has been constructed.
Conversely, every construction assembled from the displayed carriers factors through one or more of these
functors. A proposed construction fails whenever it omits one of the required data or hypotheses.
Proof. Items 1–3 are theorems 198.2 and 199.1; item 4 is theorems 200.1 and 200.2; item 5 is theorems 201.1 and 201.2;
item 6 follows from proposition 215.1 and theorem 208.1 and Tannaka reconstruction; item 7 is theorem 214.2;
item 8 is recognition Theorem 216.2, realized by theorem 218.2; item 9 is theorem 213.1; item 10 is exemplified by
theorems 211.2 and 220.1. Conversely, each operation factors by type as collision, ordered composition, exchange,
trace, sewing, reduction, pushforward, or scalar evaluation, and each appears in the atlas. The no-go constitution
records the missing input in every forbidden identification.

224.2     The single invariant sentence
The complete statement is:

        A genuinely noncommutative chiral field theory on a curve may carry two independent
        associative structures: ordered fusion in a transverse topological direction and associa-
        tive, possibly nonlocal, chiral composition on the curve. Their coherent coexistence is
        an algebra over a two-direction cofibrant operad, with locality supported on the aligned-
        decomposition sector. Bar, centre, exchange, trace, sewing, reduction, and arithmetic are
        functorial constructions from this typed datum, never substitutes for it.

224.3     Consequences of the typed datum
The typed datum has four consequences.
   (i) The aligned projector is the organizing law of mixed locality.
  (ii) It identifies the missing associative chiral/nonlocal E1 as a second operadic direction rather than a
       synonym for transverse order.
 (iii) It turns generators and relations into a derived, calculable presentation theory over the two-
       coloured cofibrant operad.
 (iv) The examples are chain-level calculations that can falsify a proposed identification as well as
      illustrate it.

224.4     Coda: the geometry remembers
The diagonal remembers singularity. The interval remembers order. The formal disc remembers asso-
ciative chiral iteration. The plane remembers exchange. The circle remembers trace. The stable curve
remembers sewing. The compactified boundary remembers the equations. A scalar remembers only
the value of a chosen functional. The typed theory keeps these memories separate until a theorem joins
them.

     Suspensions, orientations, and low-arity
                                        signs
225.1   Cohomological suspension
For homogeneous 𝑎, | 𝑠𝑎 | = | 𝑎 | + 1. The Koszul rule for exchanging homogeneous factors 𝑢, 𝑣 is

                                            𝑢 ⊗ 𝑣 ↦−→ (−1) | 𝑢 | | 𝑣 | 𝑣 ⊗ 𝑢.

The binary bar coderivation is fixed by

                                          𝑏2 ( 𝑠𝑎 ⊗ 𝑠𝑏 ) = (−1) | 𝑎 | 𝑠 ( 𝑎𝑏 ) .

On a word, moving this degree-one coderivation past the preceding suspended letters gives
                                                      𝑟
                                                      ∑︁−1
                         𝑏 2 [ 𝑎1 | · · · | 𝑎 𝑟 ] =          (−1) 𝜎𝑖 [ 𝑎1 | · · · | 𝑎𝑖 𝑎𝑖 +1 | · · · | 𝑎𝑟 ] ,
                                                      𝑖 =1

                                                              (| 𝑎𝑗 | + 1) + | 𝑎𝑖 | .
                                                      ∑︁
                                             𝜎𝑖 =
                                                      𝑗 <𝑖

For degree-zero letters the signs alternate. Any convention differing by a global suspension conjugation
produces an isomorphic complex; mixing conventions within one calculation does not.

225.2    Four-letter bar word
For ungraded letters and the alternating convention,

                           𝑏 [ 𝑎 | 𝑏 | 𝑐 | 𝑑 ] = [ 𝑎𝑏 | 𝑐 | 𝑑 ] − [ 𝑎 | 𝑏𝑐 | 𝑑 ] + [ 𝑎 | 𝑏 | 𝑐𝑑 ] .

Applying 𝑏 again gives

                                𝑏 2 [ 𝑎 | 𝑏 | 𝑐 | 𝑑 ] = [( 𝑎𝑏 ) 𝑐 | 𝑑 ] − [ 𝑎𝑏 | 𝑐𝑑 ]
                                                             − [ 𝑎 ( 𝑏𝑐 )| 𝑑 ] + [ 𝑎 | 𝑏 ( 𝑐𝑑 )]
                                                             + [ 𝑎𝑏 | 𝑐𝑑 ] − [ 𝑎 |( 𝑏𝑐 ) 𝑑 ] .

The middle terms cancel, and associativity cancels the remaining two pairs. This is the cellular boundary
of the two-dimensional associahedron in its simplest word model.


                                                                                                                     569
225. Suspensions, orientations, and low-arity signs                                               570


225.3     Two edge colours
For a bicoloured graph 𝐺 , choose

                                   or( 𝐺 ) = det 𝐸𝑋 ( 𝐺 ) ⊗ det 𝐸⊥ ( 𝐺 ) .

Contracting an 𝑋 -edge removes a basis vector from the first determinant; contracting a ⊥-edge removes
one from the second. Reversing the contraction order exchanges two odd normal directions and con-
tributes −1. Hence
                                         𝑑 𝑋 𝑑⊥ + 𝑑⊥ 𝑑 𝑋 = 0.
The same convention controls the two loop operators in the master equation.

225.4     Arnold relation and KZ curvature
For three points,
                                             𝜔𝑖𝑗 = 𝑑 log( 𝑧 𝑖 − 𝑧𝑗 )
satisfy
                                  𝜔12 ∧ 𝜔23 + 𝜔23 ∧ 𝜔31 + 𝜔31 ∧ 𝜔12 = 0.
The arity-three KZ curvature is

                        𝐹∇ = 𝜅 2 [Ω12 , Ω13 ] 𝜔12 ∧ 𝜔13 + [Ω12 , Ω23 ] 𝜔12 ∧ 𝜔23
                                             + [ Ω13 , Ω23 ] 𝜔13 ∧ 𝜔23 .
                                                                      


The infinitesimal braid relation rewrites the three commutators as one common commutator with the
coefficients in Arnold’s relation, so 𝐹∇ = 0.

225.5     Rational Yang–Baxter expansion
For 𝑅𝑖𝑗 ( 𝑢) = 1 − ℏ 𝑃𝑖𝑗 /𝑢, expand both sides of Yang–Baxter. The order-ℏ terms agree trivially. The
order-ℏ2 identity reduces, after a common denominator, to the permutation identities

                       𝑃12 𝑃13 = 𝑃23 𝑃12 ,     𝑃13 𝑃23 = 𝑃12 𝑃13 ,     𝑃12 𝑃23 = 𝑃13 𝑃12 .

The cubic term agrees because both sides represent the same longest permutation.

                                         Exact finite computations
226.1    Finite chain-matrix construction
For each total polynomial weight 𝑤 ≤ 5 and word length 𝑟 , let 𝐶𝑤,𝑟 be the normalized bar space spanned
over ℚ by words whose nonunit monomial letters have total weight 𝑤 . The bar differential is the alternat-
ing sum of adjacent multiplications. In the quantum plane at the rational parameter 𝑞 = 2, multiplication
is
                                     ( 𝑥 𝑎 𝑦 𝑏 ) ( 𝑥 𝑐 𝑦 𝑑 ) = 𝑞 𝑏𝑐 𝑥 𝑎+𝑐 𝑦 𝑏+𝑑 .
In the Jordan plane it is determined by the exact reduction 𝑦𝑥 ↦→ 𝑥𝑦 + 𝑥 2 . Thus every differential
matrix in the stated window has rational entries; its kernel, image, and rank are taken over ℚ.

226.3     The 𝔰𝔩 2 complex
The matrix in chapter 207 has determinant −4, so 𝑑2 is an isomorphism. The top differential is zero by
the Jacobi identity and direct calculation. The result is

                                  (dim 𝐻0 , dim 𝐻1 , dim 𝐻2 , dim 𝐻3 ) = (1, 0, 0, 1) .

226.4      The 𝐴 2 Hall relation at 𝑞 = 2
In the basis ( [ 𝑋 ] , [𝑌 ]),

                                𝑒12 𝑒2 = (3, 3) ,       𝑒1 𝑒2 𝑒1 = (3, 1) ,    𝑒2 𝑒12 = (3, 0) .

Therefore
                                            (3, 3) − 3(3, 1) + 2(3, 0) = (0, 0) .
The symbolic proof in theorem 210.1 holds for arbitrary 𝑞 ; the finite check catches coefficient transposi-
tions.

226.5     Finite stable-trace Jacobi calculation
For labels ( 𝑎, 𝑏 ) with 0 ≤ 𝑎, 𝑏 ≤ 3 and 𝑎 + 𝑏 > 0, evaluate all ordered triples under

                                [( 𝑎, 𝑏 ) , ( 𝑐, 𝑑 )] = ( 𝑎𝑑 − 𝑏𝑐 ) ( 𝑎 + 𝑐 − 1, 𝑏 + 𝑑 − 1) .

227.1    Quadratic transverse presentation
Given coefficient generators 𝑉 ∈ FactD( 𝑋 ) and relations 𝑅 → 𝑉 ⊗! 𝑉 , the strict quadratic algebra is

                                           𝐴 = 𝑇⊗! (𝑉 )/( 𝑅) .

The derived presentation is the homotopy pushout

                                                        𝜕0
                                      FreeE1 ( 𝑅)            FreeE1 (𝑉 )
                                                        𝜕1


                                           1                   𝐴der .

A cofibrant replacement adds syzygy cells whenever relations among relations occur.

227.2    Associative chiral presentation
For formal-disc generators 𝑀 and ordered field relations 𝑅 𝑋 , replace FreeE1 by FreeAssch . A relation can
                                                                                                  𝑋
be a finite-pole identity
                                                                                             !
                                                        𝑓𝑖 ( 𝑧 − 𝑤 )𝑌 ( 𝑏 𝑖 , 𝑤 )𝑌 ( 𝑎𝑖 , 𝑧 ) = 0.

A quantum relation includes the S-matrix coefficients. The derived quotient remembers a null-
homotopy of this field identity and its compatibility with nested substitutions.

227.3    Doubly associative presentation
Use the two-coloured operad P. The data consist of

                                          (𝑉 ; 𝑅⊥ , 𝑅 𝑋 , 𝑅mix ) ,

where 𝑅mix contains the rectangular locality square and any braided permutation between middle factors.
Resolving the presentation automatically adds:
    • transverse overlap cells;


                                                                                                       575
227. Generators-and-relations templates                                                           576


      • chiral collision overlap cells;
      • mixed prisms comparing the two reduction orders;
      • higher cells filling the resulting spheres.
This is the promised model-category implementation of homotopically complete generators and rela-
tions.

227.4      Relative presentation
For a semisimple base 𝑆 , use free 𝑆 -bimodule algebras and relative tensor products throughout. The
quiver template is
                             𝐴 = 𝑇𝑆 ( 𝑀 )/( 𝑅) ,     Bar 𝑆 ( 𝐴) = 𝑆 ⊗𝕃𝐴 𝑆.
A coloured chiral quiver replaces every 𝑒𝑗 𝑀 𝑒 𝑖 by a coefficient object and imposes the factorisation
compatibility on every source-target component.

     Logical status of comparison statements
A comparison is used only after its source, target, carrier category, and map have been specified. A finite
chain-matrix calculation establishes its stated finite window only. A formal identity carries no analytic
convergence claim, and a conditional comparison is invoked only with its named hypothesis. The ob-
structions in Chapter 222 and the exact calculations in Appendix 226 give the corresponding negative
and finite criteria.
